# QA log

One row per shipped stamp, recording whether the QA pass CLAUDE.md requires
BEFORE a merge actually ran, and what it found.

`flutter/test/qa_record_test.dart` fails on the real runner when the current
`updateStamp` has no row here. That is the point: the check runs on every push
to a `claude/**` branch, so a stamp cannot reach main without someone having
had to look at this file and write in it.

What this guard can and cannot do, said plainly so nobody mistakes a green
check for more than it is:

- It CAN stop a QA pass being forgotten. That is the failure it was built for,
  and the failure that put a broken monthly cap on the founder's phone in
  f2.71 for two hours.
- It CANNOT stop a row being written for a pass that did not happen. Nothing
  automated can. What it does is turn an omission into a deliberate false
  sentence sitting in a diff, which is a different and much harder thing to do
  by accident.

A row of SKIPPED is a valid and expected entry. It is worth far more than a
missing row: it says what happened. Two of the rows below say exactly that,
backfilled honestly rather than left blank or quietly marked green.

| f2.73 | qa-tester | 3 MUST FIX and 4 SHOULD FIX fixed and re-checked before merge; 4 lower findings deferred and named below | Utang person depth. MUST: two people sharing a name were merged, so the statement sent to one billed her for the other's ₱7,000; the SETTLED row printed what was LEFT while calling it paid; huge amounts saturated at the int64 ceiling and printed a credible wrong peso figure into a friend's chat app. CORRECTED after session 14: this row first also claimed the settled row read a stored amount of "2400" as ₱0. It cannot. Every load runs through sanitizeData, so a screen never sees a string amount, and the two test assertions guarding that claim passed against the broken code. The other half of the finding, printing what was LEFT while calling it paid, was real and is fixed. SHOULD: whole peso rounding made the document visibly fail to add up; the same payment showed two different amounts in the history and the statement; every payment was listed twice; a failed share did nothing at all, silently. DEFERRED: nameOf and utangAging are still two rules that can disagree on whitespace-only or duplicate-id people; the sheet is O(R×P) per rebuild; an imported overpaid row can make Remind and the statement disagree; a year past 9999 loses the As of date (RN parity). |
| f2.74 | qa-tester | 3 MUST FIX and 5 SHOULD FIX fixed and re-checked before merge; 2 lower findings deferred and named below | Period selector on Activity. MUST: the Show all button left the period on, so an empty filtered list was a dead end while the sentence beside it said the entries were only hidden; the date picker asserted and crashed on any device clock before 1995, because firstDate was a constant while lastDate came from the clock; two of the new tests were going to start failing on 1 August 2026, four days out, and turn the branch check red on main (four more by January 2027; the commit message and the first version of this row both said three, corrected after session 14 re-proved the count in a pre-fix worktree), because the History-mounted selector fell back to the real clock while the fixtures were pinned to July. SHOULD: the clear X was a 16dp target sitting on a 48dp button that opens a full screen calendar when you miss it; a backwards custom range silently matched nothing; a future dated entry was unreachable in Month mode; Period.copyWith could not clear a bound and had no callers; the shift goldens never checked what a custom period preserves, proven by breaking it. Also fixed while open: Reports pushed Activity without the month the person had already chosen. DEFERRED: tapping the already selected Month chip is inert so there is no one tap return to today; the empty state does not name the active period as the thing hiding rows. |
| f2.75 | qa-tester | 2 MUST FIX and 4 SHOULD FIX fixed and re-checked before merge; 3 lower findings deferred and named below | Quick add editor. MUST: seeding the presets when the editor OPENED flipped hasData true on an empty app, which permanently replaced Menu's BRING YOUR DATA OVER card, the only in-app route to import from the old app, with a backup card, so every RN tester lost their migration prompt by looking at a settings sheet (fixed by seeding on the first real edit instead); neither write was wrapped, so a refused write froze the sheet on Saving... with no message and let the exception escape unhandled. SHOULD: a long label clipped the amount off the Budget chip so the button logged a figure never shown (24 character cap); an amount of 0.004 was accepted and drew as ₱0 while every tap filed a real expense (0.01 floor); a comma grouped amount got the wrong sentence when the app itself prints ₱1,500 everywhere (its own message now); the sheet had no safe area and no height cap, so past about 13 presets the grab handle sat under the status bar and Add and Done were below the fold. The two acceptance divergences from RN are recorded in the goldens on purpose rather than left to be discovered. DEFERRED: an RN backup where the person deleted every quick add still resurrects the four defaults, because no RN backup carries the edited flag; two Edit taps in one frame can stack two sheets (idempotent, no data harm); the amount field's only screen reader label is its hint. |
| f2.76 | play-launch-auditor, then qa-tester | 2 BLOCKER and 6 FAIL from the launch audit, then 4 MUST FIX and 4 SHOULD FIX from a second pass on the same stamp, all fixed and re-checked before merge; 5 lower findings deferred and named below | The home screen widget, and the first NATIVE release in many stamps, so the release path was the cold path. BLOCKER: the layout used <Space>, which RemoteViews refuses to inflate because it carries no @RemoteView annotation, so the tile would have drawn "Problem loading widget" on every Android version including the picker preview, frozen in res/ and costing a second manual install. BLOCKER: the stamp was not bumped, so the publisher would have cut the release, uploaded the APK, then failed its own duplicate-stamp check, written NO delivery row, opened an issue falsely saying nothing shipped, never told the founder to install, and left the watchdog reporting in step while the phone silently stopped receiving every future patch. FAIL: the Log bar did not log, because nothing in Dart read the launch URI, while the picker description promising a one tap Log button is frozen in res/. FAIL: content came to roughly 176dp inside a tile declaring 110dp, and the Log bar is the last child so it clips first. FAIL: three permissions arrive with the widget plumbing that the privacy receipt screen did not list, on a card promising the complete list. FAIL: the branch check never compiled Android, so the first Android build of a native change would have happened on main inside the publisher, after the merge. Fixed: Space to TextView with a guard listing the @RemoteView classes, stamp bumped, launch URI wired through shell.dart under the same guards as the first-log prompt, heights reduced and the headline cap lowered, privacy receipt corrected, and a gated `flutter build apk --debug` added to the branch check. DEFERRED and named: no previewImage so Android 11 and 12 pickers fall back to the app icon; the publisher's install shout is gated on a step that can fail before it; the nothing-shipped issue text is wrong on a release run; the watchdog cannot see Mode at all. SECOND PASS, qa-tester: Second pass on the SAME stamp, after the launch audit above, because a native release cannot be corrected over the air and the audit had looked at the release path rather than the pixels. It found four more, and two of them were the whole feature. MUST: in light mode the entire tile was invisible, contrast 1.02 to 1, because Dart pushed the currently resolved palette while widget_bg.xml fixes the card dark on every phone. The default appearance is 'system' and most Android phones ship light, so that was the DEFAULT experience; it survived because the existing test forced dark in setUp and the one light test only asked whether light DIFFERED from dark, which the bug satisfied. MUST: the one tap Log button worked exactly once per app process, because the tap was parked in a static field whose only reader was ShellScreen.initState, and the shell sits inside a keyless ListenableBuilder so its Element is reused forever. MUST: that unread tap then survived in memory and fired at the next shell mount, which on a fresh install is the instant onboarding finishes, stacking a second log sheet under the welcome one. MUST: the tile printed a different number from Home for the same instant, ₱413 where Home said ₱412.50, because it used the whole peso formatter, which also rounds UP and flattened 40 centavos a day to a bare ₱0. SHOULD: every explanatory sentence on the tile was cut mid-word, including both recovery instructions, at 43 to 66 characters in a row holding about 37; HomeTile.ready hung off a .then so one failed disk write silenced the tile for the whole session, including the push that clears it after Start fresh; app lock auto-disabling when biometrics vanish silently republished the daily number to the home screen; and every 600ms of typing in the notes editor cost ten platform writes plus a launcher redraw. Fixed: the tile now always takes the chosen theme's DARK palette with a contrast test over all eight themes at both brightnesses, the shell registers a live consumer and releases it on dispose, the two first-frame sheets became one, formatMoney moved to money/format.dart so the tile and Home share one function, copy capped at 36 characters with a test, whenComplete, widgetHideAmount carried over when app lock is force-disabled, and push skips an unchanged map. Also fixed while open: home_order_test's crunch fixture was date dependent under a header claiming it was clock free, and had been failing on the runner since the 29th. DEFERRED and named: cycle.dart never carries a payday into the quiet and committed states so one copy branch is unreachable; the private day formatter is only compared to Home's on a single date; asOf is set on two states that name no figure; the Log bar's outline stroke is a fixed Barako orange while its label takes the theme accent; and the widget picker still has no previewImage. |
| f2.77 | founder on the phone, then qa-tester on the fix | 1 MUST FIX found by the founder, fixed with two guards; 4 deferred findings from earlier rows cleared alongside it | The founder installed the f2.76 base APK, logged an expense, and the "Starbucks ₱220 logged. Undo" receipt sat on the screen forever, across every tab. Root cause: Flutter's SnackBar defaults `persist` to `action != null`, so every snackbar in the app offering Undo ignored the duration written directly above it. Seven of them, on the most used write paths there are. Fixed by making each one say out loud whether it stays, and one deliberately does: the note that failed to save, because a message reporting a lost note timing out after four seconds is how somebody loses a note. Guarded twice, both proven failing first: a widget test that logs an expense and watches the receipt leave, and a source scan that refuses any SnackBar carrying an action without a persist decision. Cleared from the deferred list at the same time: nameOf and utangAging resolved a person by two different rules, so a receivable whose person field was whitespace showed 500 on the person row and opened an empty sheet, and a duplicate person id in a backup resolved to opposite records; an overpaid utang made the Statement and the Remind message name two different amounts to the same friend, the document naming the smaller one, and the statement now floors each utang separately (a recorded divergence from RN, whose global netting quietly credits one debt against another); the tile's private day formatter and its third copy of the month names are gone, both now import the shared prettyDay, and the test that claimed to compare them and in fact asserted a tautology was replaced; and cycleStatus now carries the payday into the quiet and committed states, which makes a branch of the tile copy that could never render actually render. |
| f2.78 | self review against the design doc, adversarial, no screens involved | 1 real defect found and fixed before any screen depended on it; every guard proven failing first | Delivery A of docs/features/unified-financial-accounts.md: the account taxonomy and the institution catalog, with no UI at all. Shipped invisibly on purpose, because it is the cheapest possible place to find out the data design is wrong, and it immediately was. THE DEFECT: the first version claimed in a comment that assets "carry no type at all" and derived every one of them to "something else". They do carry a type. screens/accounts.dart has offered crypto, stocks, MP2, real estate, vehicle and other since it shipped, so the first screen that grouped assets would have read a crypto holding and a house as the same thing. The comment was written from memory and the picker was three files away, which is the same failure mode CLAUDE.md already records twice: when a comment describes what the code does, read the code. Guarded by a scan that reads the real picker list off disk and fails if it and the mapping disagree in either direction, proven by adding an NFT option and watching it go red. OTHER GUARDS, each proven failing first: the golden key-set contract, by making one new field unconditional and watching nine RN fixtures reject the key; and the wrong-collection check, by trusting a stored subtype blindly and watching a property row classify as a spendable account. NOT DONE, deliberately: kind is still clamped to its four legacy values, nothing is backfilled onto user data, no migration runs, and no screen reads any of this yet. The only behaviour change a person could notice is that junk in one of the eleven new field names is now stripped on load rather than carried, and nothing writes those names yet. |
| f2.79 | founder on the phone, then a rendered specimen sheet compared by eye | 1 MUST FIX, found by the founder and confirmed by rendering; fixed with three guards proven failing first | The founder said the old React Native app's font looked better than the Flutter one and asked whether it could be adapted. Two findings. FIRST, the old app loads no custom font at all, so every screen there is the plain Android system face, which means "adapt the RN font" and "keep Salapify's own type" were in tension and needed a founder decision, not a guess. SECOND and this is the real one, the preference had a defect underneath it: Fraunces, the display serif used for every big figure, draws the peso sign with a long crossbar, and beside a minus sign the two run together into what reads as a line struck through the number. Negative ₱720 rendered as a crossed-out ₱720, on every negative figure in the app, and the founder's own screenshot of Home showed it. Diagnosed by rendering the same figures in four candidate faces at the real size on the real background rather than by arguing, and the founder chose one font everywhere by looking at it. Guards, each proven failing first: a scan that fails on any file naming a font family directly, which found twelve hardcoded families in the two share-image screens that no ordinary render would ever have shown, proven by hardcoding one in overview.dart and watching it report the exact line; the display face must be a family the pubspec actually bundles, because a typo there falls back to the platform font silently; and the face must ship the weight the hero numbers ask for, since a missing weight is synthesised rather than refused. NOT DONE, deliberately: the Fraunces files stay bundled and unreferenced. Removing them changes the asset bundle, which Shorebird will not patch, so it would cost the founder a second manual install for 100KB. It rides along with the next native change. |
| f2.80 | self review plus the render harness, which found both real defects | 2 MUST FIX, both found by LOOKING at the rendered screen and neither visible in the code; every guard proven failing first | Delivery B of docs/features/unified-financial-accounts.md: one Add button replacing "+ Account" and "+ Asset", asking what is being added and routing the answer to whichever of the three collections can actually record it. DEFECT 1: the details form still showed the Kind chips after the person had just chosen a subtype one screen earlier, so picking "Payroll account" and then flipping the chip to Cash stored kind cash next to subtype payroll_account, an account that disagrees with its own classification, which no engine would notice and no screen would ever explain. DEFECT 2: the new institution picker sat directly above the old free text "Bank or brand" field, asking the same question twice, in different words, with two different answers and nothing saying which one counted. Both were invisible in the code, because the two rows are two hundred lines apart in a nine hundred line file and each reads correctly on its own. Removing them also brought the form from six fields to four and put the Save button back above the fold. Guards, each proven failing first: the routing itself, broken by sending every answer to the accounts form and watching a car become spendable cash and a credit card never reach the debts form; the Kind rule, broken by showing the chips always and watching the seeded case fail while the edit case stayed green, which is the half that matters because hiding them everywhere would strand every pre-existing account with no way to correct its kind; and the bank question, asserted as exactly one field. Also fixed a test that would have lied: tester.tap on an off-screen widget does not throw, it dispatches where the widget is not and does nothing, so the assertion fifty lines later fails with an empty list and no hint. Every tap in the new file scrolls first. NOT DONE, deliberately: no money math changed, no row moves between collections, the golden-locked debt engine is untouched and the classification is merged onto the row it produced rather than threaded through it, and an edit never writes a classification because this sheet does not ask what an existing row is. |
| f2.81 | self review plus the render harness, which found the one real defect | 1 fixed, plus a design rule that needed a guard in BOTH directions; every guard proven failing first | Delivery C of docs/features/unified-financial-accounts.md: the Accounts list regrouped by the taxonomy category rather than the four legacy kinds, with a subtotal per section and the institution initials on rows that have no emoji. Debts now split into CREDIT CARDS, LOANS and INSTALLMENTS instead of one undifferentiated DEBTS block, each with its own red subtotal, and the "manage debts elsewhere" note appears once at the bottom instead of under every debt group. Empty sections are hidden, so a new person sees one honest empty line rather than six headings over six "nothing here yet" lines. THE DEFECT: UnionBank rendered as UN on its circle, which reads as the United Nations. Fixed by splitting a run-together name at its internal capitals, which corrects GoTyme, SeaBank, GrabPay, ShopeePay and EastWest in the same rule rather than five special cases, while an all-capitals name like BPI or RCBC keeps its first two letters. Invisible in code; obvious in the render. THE RULE THAT NEEDED TWO GUARDS: CLAUDE.md says Salapify never replaces an icon the USER picked, and an account icon is user data that lives in the backup file. So the initials appear ONLY where the icon field is genuinely empty, and the save path stops writing the default money emoji when a bank was chosen, because writing it would make "left blank" and "picked the money emoji" the same stored value and the row would have to guess. Guarded in both directions, proven by preferring the bank always and watching a typed emoji disappear, with the pre-existing-account case staying green. NOT DONE, deliberately: no money math changed, netWorthParts is untouched, debt rows are not tappable from this screen because editing a debt belongs to the screen that owns interest and due dates, and no row moves between collections. |
| f2.82 | self review against the design doc's own risk ranking, plus the render | 0 defects reaching the phone; the whole batch IS the fix for a wrong number that adding the field alone would have created | Delivery D of docs/features/unified-financial-accounts.md, and the item that document ranks as its most dangerous. An account can now carry its own currency. The reason this is a money-math change and not a picker: every engine in the app adds balances as if they were one currency, because until now they were, so the moment a dollar account can exist a $1,000 balance lands in a peso net worth as ₱1,000 and understates the truth by roughly ₱55,000 with nothing on screen to hint at it. A missing feature is visible; a wrong total is not. So the field ships WITH the rule: a row whose currency differs from the base is excluded from netWorthParts and from safeToSpend, and the screen NAMES what was left out, because excluding silently is the same failure wearing a different mask. The safeToSpend half matters more than net worth: that figure reaches Home and the home screen widget, so counting a dollar balance as pesos there would tell somebody they can spend money they do not have. Both are recorded divergences from the React Native engines, which have no per-account currency, and every golden vector still matches byte for byte because no vector carries one. Guards, each proven failing first: net worth stops skipping and a ₱5,000 total reads ₱6,000; the daily number stops skipping; and absent-means-base is inverted, which would empty every total in the app on the day it shipped. The rule is also tested with the base set to USD, so a hardcoded "skip dollars" could not pass. UI, from looking: the currency control was eight chips first, which is two wrapped rows for a question whose answer is the base currency nearly every time, and it pushed Save off the bottom of the sheet, the same defect f2.80 fixed by removing the Kind chips; it is one row now. The exclusion notice was at the BOTTOM of the list, a full scroll from the number it explains; it sits under the net worth card now. NOT DONE, deliberately: no conversion. That needs the honest-total rules in section 5 of the design document plus golden vectors, because it changes a number, and none of it is needed to stop the total being wrong today. |
| f2.83 | self review plus two widget tests that drove the real screen | 1 CRASH found and fixed before it could ship; 1 wrong-number defect found by a test I nearly did not write; every rule proven failing first | Delivery E of docs/features/unified-financial-accounts.md, the piece that document gates separately because it can produce a WRONG number rather than a missing one. A foreign account now converts into the base currency, under the four rules the document set: a fresh rate converts and names itself, a stale one converts and says how old it is, a manual one is used and labelled as the person's own, and no rate at all excludes, names the currency and offers a way to price it. THE CRASH: the manual rate dialog held its TextEditingController in the async function that opened it and disposed it the moment showDialog returned, while the dialog was still animating out and its field was still reading it. Tapping "Use this rate" threw "A TextEditingController was used after being disposed" and took the frame down. Nothing in the code reads as wrong; only driving the real screen found it. The dialog owns its own controller now. THE WRONG NUMBER: with conversion working, net worth included the converted dollars while the row underneath still said "not counted" and the section subtotal still excluded them. Two versions of one number, on the same screen, six lines apart. The row now shows the converted equivalent and the subtotal counts exactly what the total counts, from the same resolution. ARCHITECTURE, deliberately: the rate table is passed in, not a module global. baseCurrencySymbol is a global and that is fine for a SIGN, where being wrong for a frame is cosmetic; a rate is arithmetic, and a total that depends on hidden state set somewhere else is how a money app gets "it was right yesterday". netWorthParts takes it as an optional argument defaulting to no conversion, which is exactly what it did before, and is why every golden vector still passes untouched. safeToSpend still refuses to convert, permanently and correctly: you cannot buy lunch with a dollar savings account today. NINETEEN vectors pin every branch, and three were proven by breaking the code: a live rate silently overriding a manual one, a converted total with nothing said about it, and a foreign debt landing on the asset side. |
| f2.84 | the retrospective itself, which is where both findings came from | 1 user-facing copy defect fixed, and the reason it was invisible fixed underneath it | The lunch and learn for the eight deliveries of 2026-07-29, written up in docs/lunch-and-learn.md. THE FINDING OF THE DAY: every screenshot this project has ever taken was of an EMPTY app. test/screens_shot.dart called SharedPreferences.setMockInitialValues({}) inside shoot(), so sixteen images across eight screens and two brightnesses were all first-run welcome states, and not one had ever contained a peso figure. The rule in CLAUDE.md that says look at the screen before shipping a screen was being followed faithfully against a fixture that could not show a money defect, which is how the crossed-out peso sign sat on Home through dozens of renders and reached the founder. Fixed with a lived-in fixture: four accounts with odd balances, twelve categorised expenses, income, a card and a loan, someone who owes and someone who is owed, a goal part way there and a logging streak, all dated relative to a fixed day so the images do not churn with the calendar. It paid for itself immediately, twice. Insights had been printing "(payday 2026-07-30)", a machine date in a sentence, on the screen whose whole job is reading plainly, while every other screen said "Jul 30"; fixed and guarded by a scan that refuses a stored ISO date interpolated into a string, proven by putting the bug back and watching it name the exact line. And the first version of the fixture used a plain category string that no engine reads, so the Budget breakdown silently grouped by label; caught by reading money/budget.dart rather than trusting the picture, which is the same lesson one level down. CLAUDE.md's render paragraph now leads with the fixture rather than the habit, because the habit was never the part that failed. |
| f2.85 | the contrast sweep itself, which is the QA pass in machine form | 2 real palette defects fixed; 1 rule of my own thrown out for being wrong | Closes open lesson 1 from today's retrospective: nobody opens the light renders, so a contrast defect there would survive exactly the way the crossed-out peso did. Closed with a measurement rather than another instruction to look. test/palette_contrast_test.dart sweeps all sixteen palettes, every theme at both brightnesses, over thirteen text-on-surface pairs the app actually draws, and holds each to WCAG AA. It is pure arithmetic over the registry, so it is total, fast, and cannot be fooled by which screens somebody thought to render. THE RULE I GOT WRONG: the first version held the card border against the page to WCAG's 3.0 for a meaningful non-text boundary, and it failed on ALL SIXTEEN palettes at ratios from 1.13 to 1.93. A check that fails on every single case is usually a wrong check, and it was: 1.4.11 covers a boundary that is the only way to identify a control, and Salapify's cards are identified by their fill. Holding it would have demanded a hard outline around every card in the app, which is a different product and not a more accessible one. Replaced with a floor that says what it is: the card must be a visibly different surface from the page, which is not a WCAG rule and is labelled as not one. THE TWO REAL DEFECTS: warningStrong measured 4.4996 against Barako dark's card, under AA by four ten-thousandths, and 4.33 against Ember dark's, genuinely under. That colour carries the 13pt section subtotals on Accounts, small enough that the 4.5 bar really applies. Lifted two points and five points respectively, which is imperceptible and clears the bar. FOUNDER-VISIBLE: two theme colours changed, both by an amount no eye can see, both in the negative-amount red. Guards proven failing first: the sweep was shown catching a plausible "make the muted grey softer" edit to Barako LIGHT at 2.69 to 1, and there is a separate test asserting the maths against WCAG's own published constants, so a mistake in the formula surfaces as a failure rather than as a permissive suite that passes everything. Open lesson 2 (the render harness is still opt-in, CI proves it does not crash rather than that what it drew is readable) is NOT closed by this and stays on the list. |
| f2.86 | the sweep itself, which is the QA pass in machine form, plus a real break of a live screen | 0 defects found in the app (every screen already passes); the finding is that the checks had to be proven twice before one of them was real | Closes open lesson 2 from the 2026-07-29 retrospective, the last one left open: the render harness is opt-in, CI runs it with --update-goldens which proves only that it does not crash, and nothing ever checked what it drew. test/screen_readability_test.dart takes the same lived-in fixture and the same ten screens, throws the pictures away, and asserts the three things about "readable" a machine can actually judge: nothing overflows, the screen drew real words, and no sentence runs off the side of the phone. It does all three at 1.0x AND 1.5x system font, and it SCROLLS, checking every screenful rather than the top of the list. It is an ordinary *_test.dart, so unlike the render it runs on the branch check with everything else. Deliberately NOT pixel comparison: golden matching is exactly why screens_shot.dart is kept out of flutter test, because it is font and platform dependent and fails on a runner for reasons that say nothing about the app. Layout metrics from the same TTF are deterministic, so measuring the layout is safe where photographing it is not. One brightness only, because a palette cannot move a box and colour is already measured exhaustively by f2.85's contrast sweep. THE PART WORTH RECORDING: my first proof that the off-the-side check could fire did not fire. It wrapped a 900 wide box in a vertical scroll view, which hands its child the viewport width, so the 900 was quietly clamped to 390 and nothing was ever off the side. The check said nothing and was right to. Had that case been written the usual way, as an assertion that the app passes, it would have read as proof and been a check measuring air. Rewritten with a Positioned label pinned past the edge, it fires. Five proofs are kept permanently, including two that guard the guard: a defect placed three phone-heights down, which is the only thing standing between the scroll sweep and a silent no-op, and a horizontal chip row that must NOT be reported, because a guard that flags every sideways list gets switched off and is then not there for the real defect. Proven on real code too, not only synthetics: one Expanded was removed from a row near the bottom of Menu, and the sweep named it at both font sizes, at the exact scroll offset, with both the overflow and the off-the-side checks firing. Every screen in the app passes today, at both sizes, all the way down. NOT DONE, deliberately: no app code changed, no money math, no UI. This is a guard only. |
| f2.87 | a re-read of the publisher workflow itself, which is what found both the stale lesson and the real hole | 1 carried-forward open lesson found to be FALSE and corrected; 1 genuine silent-failure hole closed; every guard proven failing first | Open lesson 3 said "a Shorebird step failure is still silent" and had been carried forward, unchanged, since session 1. It is not true and has not been for several sessions: the "Say plainly that nothing shipped" step opens a GitHub issue on any non-zero step in the publisher. Nobody had re-read the workflow; the lesson was restated on trust in every retrospective since. That is precisely the mistake CLAUDE.md names one paragraph away, when a rule describes what a tool does, read the tool and not the rule, and it means a carried lesson costs something rather than being free. Corrected in place in docs/lunch-and-learn.md rather than quietly deleted. THE HOLE THAT WAS REAL, and it is a different shape from the one written down: a Shorebird step that SUCCEEDS having shipped nothing. On a patch run the patch number is parsed with `|| true`, and that `|| true` is load-bearing, its absence cost a whole delivery once, because a release run has no "Published Patch" line anywhere in its log. The price of having it is that on a patch run an empty patch number is indistinguishable from success at the exit-code level, so the next step writes a delivery row reading "patch: none" while the phone received nothing. That is the worst possible failure here specifically, because a row in docs/delivery-log.md is the ONE thing this project treats as proof that something shipped, and a row that means nothing is worse than no row because it is believed. Checked against history: `none` appears three times in the log and all three are `release` rows, where it is correct. The hole has never fired. THE FIX, and the part that matters more than the fix: the decision was pulled out of the YAML into .github/scripts/verify-shipped.sh, which runs BEFORE the delivery row is written and fails through the same nothing-shipped issue as any other failure. Every lesson about delivery in this project has landed as a line of YAML that nothing could run, and a workflow step is only ever tested by shipping, which is the most expensive test bench available. flutter/test/publisher_guard_test.dart now drives that script through eleven cases on the branch check, before the merge. DELIBERATELY NOT CHECKED: the wording of a successful release log. Nobody here has read one closely enough to quote it, and a check written against a guessed string would fail on every release forever, which is how a guard gets switched off. The APK is what the founder installs, so its existence and size are what the release branch asserts. Guards proven failing first, twice on real files: the wiring broken by a one-character typo in the script name, caught by name ("the publisher no longer calls verify-shipped.sh at all"), and the guard MOVED to after the recording step, caught by the ordering assertion, which is the half that matters because a check that runs after the row is written cannot stop the row existing. The silent half is proven too: a release run with no patch number must PASS, because that is the normal case and a guard that reddened every base APK build would be removed within a day. NOT DONE, deliberately: no app code changed, no money math, no UI. |
| f2.88 | looking at the rendered screens with real data in them, which is what found it | 1 user-facing copy defect fixed across six sentences, 1 grammar defect, and the fixture gap that hid both; guard proven failing first | FOUND BY LOOKING, which is the point. With the lived-in fixture finally reaching the fullest Insights render, one number appeared three times on one screen in two spellings: the hero read PHP26,525.25, the sentence under it read "About PHP26,525.25 a day for the next 1 day", and a card a few centimetres below read "about PHP26,525 free to move". A sentence that says "about" and then prints centavos is arguing with itself, and the second card was already correct, so this was never a missing convention. Steady Pay rounds. The Home pace line rounds. It was applied at some call sites and not others, which is the class of defect no test catches and no reader notices until the day they are looking at their own money. THE FIX: formatMoneyAbout in money/format.dart, applied at six sites across Insights, Reports and Debts, plus two more that already rounded via a bare .roundToDouble() and now say what they mean at the call site. It rounds to NEAREST, deliberately, and the comment says why: formatMoneyText rounds UP, which is right for its own contexts and wrong here, because rounding a spending figure up tells somebody they have more room than they do, every time, in the one direction that costs them money. THE GRAMMAR: "for the next 1 day" read like a machine, and on the last day of a cycle "a day" describes nothing anyway, since one day left means the rate and the amount are the same number. It now reads "About PHP26,525 to reach tomorrow, your Jul 30 payday." SCOPE, deliberately narrow: the hero figures are UNTOUCHED. Home's per-day hero is coupled to the home screen widget on purpose, and there is a written incident behind it, the tile once used the whole-peso formatter and printed 413 where Home said 412.50, on the one surface that cannot be corrected without a reinstall. Balances, totals, logged amounts and anything the user typed stay exact to the centavo, because the golden vectors pin them there and a rounded balance is a wrong balance rather than a friendly one. THE FIXTURE GAP UNDERNEATH IT: f2.84 gave shoot() a lived-in store, and the shots that build their own store quietly kept their thin ones. Only 1 of 22 store setups in the harness used it. insights-full-dark, the fullest render of the screen that reasons about somebody's money, was seeded with one cash account, one grocery expense and one credit card, so it showed "Money health 10 of 100" from a store with no income in it and nobody could tell whether that was the app judging a real person harshly or an artefact. Switched to the lived-in fixture. The remaining empty-store shots were checked one by one and left alone on purpose: onboarding, the sample-data banner and the icon catalogues are first-run or constructed by nature, where an empty store is the correct fixture. A FALSE ALARM WORTH RECORDING: the investigation started from Home and Insights appearing to disagree about the payday date and the daily figure. They did not. The two shots were rendered from two different fixtures. The engines were probed directly before anything was changed and all three agreed, payday 2026-07-30, 1 day, PHP26,525.25. Guard: test/about_money_rounding_test.dart, a scanner refusing "about" followed by the centavo formatter, proven by putting the bug back into the live Insights line and watching it name lib/screens/insights.dart:789, plus six unit tests including the nearest-not-up distinction and the non-finite values a restored backup can carry, since roundToDouble throws on infinity and the order of the guards inside the helper is load-bearing. |
| f2.89 | looking at the screens again, then unifying the fixture so screens could be compared to each other at all | 1 user-facing defect fixed; 3 suspected defects investigated and proven NOT to be defects; 1 new machine check, scoped down after its first run proved the strict version wrong | THE SWEEP THAT FOUND NOTHING, first. Three things looked wrong across the rendered screens and all three were artefacts of the harness rather than the app. Home and Insights appeared to disagree about both the payday date and the daily figure, which would have been serious; the engines were probed directly and all agreed, payday 2026-07-30, 1 day, PHP26,525.25, and the two shots were simply drawn from two different fixtures. Accounts read "Total owed PHP60,400" against Utang's "PHP60,480.40", an 80 peso 40 centavo gap; same cause, two different fixtures. Every account row showed a list-like glyph where an icon belongs; that is the missing-emoji box for the money emoji, which CLAUDE.md names explicitly and says never to fix. Three investigations, no bugs, and the reason all three were false is one thing: 21 of the 22 store setups in the render harness built their own private person, so no two screens could ever be compared. Cross-screen comparison is the most valuable kind of looking there is and it was impossible by construction. THE FIX FOR THAT: the lived-in fixture grew a foreign-currency account, a vehicle, and a manual USD rate, and the Accounts shot now uses it instead of its own near-copy. Accounts and Utang now render the same debts and agree to the centavo, PHP60,480. Growing the fixture rather than shrinking it is the rule CLAUDE.md already states. A side benefit worth naming: per-account currency is the most dangerous money code in the app by the design document's own ranking, the one place that can produce a WRONG total rather than a missing one, and it was rendered on exactly one screen. It now appears on every screen in every render, and the rule that spendable money permanently excludes it is visible on Home rather than merely tested. IT PAID FOR ITSELF IMMEDIATELY, exactly as the f2.84 fixture did. With a savings target finally present in the fixture, the Accounts row rendered "Savings account - BPI - 49% of PHP1..." and cut off the target, which is the one number that line exists to show. The comment directly above the code says a savings target REPLACES the identity line because a third clause would not fit on one line at any font size. The code appended. The author knew and wrote it anyway, and it survived because no fixture had ever given an account a target. Fixed by doing what the comment says; nothing is lost, since the subtype is the heading the row already sits under and the institution is drawn as an avatar two centimetres to the left. THE NEW CHECK, and the part where I was wrong first: cut-off text is invisible to every existing check. An ellipsized line fits, throws nothing, stays inside the frame, and keeps its full string in the widget, so a test asserting on the text passes while the phone shows "PHP1...". Only the render knows. The first version flagged everything at 1.5x system font too, including a search hint and two account names, and that version was wrong: an ellipsis at 1.5x is a layout degrading gracefully, which is what an ellipsis is for. Scoped to default font size, where an ellipsis means the layout cannot fit its own ordinary content for everybody. Proven by putting the append back and watching it name the exact string, "Savings account - BPI - 49% of PHP100,000 is cut off". NOT DONE, deliberately: no money math, no engine, no golden vector touched. |
| f2.90 | looking at the rendered screens, again, and then testing whether the guard that should have caught it actually could | 1 user-facing defect fixed; 1 EXISTING guard found to be a list wearing the title of a rule; 1 fixture blind spot closed | THE DEFECT: the Owed to me list printed "Due 2026-08-15" under a person's name. A stored machine date in a sentence, on the screen about asking a friend for money, where reading like a spreadsheet is the last thing wanted. It is the same defect class f2.84 fixed on Insights. The helper it needed was already in utang.dart, ten lines above the offending line, with its own comment explaining why a utang list in particular needs the year when it is not this one. THE GUARD THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT, and this is the finding worth more than the fix: test/no_iso_dates_in_copy_test.dart is titled "no user-facing string interpolates a raw stored date" and it matched four literal key names, payday, dueDate, targetDate and interestThroughISO. Exactly the four that existed on the day the bug was found. This key is called oldestDue, so the guard read as a rule and implemented a list, and the next offender is by definition the spelling nobody thought of. Widened to match by SHAPE, anything ending in Date, Due or ISO, plus payday. Ending in Day deliberately does NOT match, because dueDay and statementDay hold a day number from 1 to 31 and printing one is correct; a pattern that caught those would be demanding a bug, and that half is asserted so the widening stays honest. THE DEEPER FIX: a static scan can only ever guess at names. screen_readability_test.dart now checks what was actually DRAWN, refusing any rendered text containing a four-two-two date, which no spelling can slip past. TWO FIXTURE BLIND SPOTS, both of which made the render-level check a no-op at first. Every receivable in the lived-in store was already overdue, so the list only ever rendered "Overdue N days" and the branch that prints the due date was unreachable: a fixture where every case is the same case proves one case. A future-dated receivable was added. And the sweep only ever looked at the FIRST face of each screen, so the Owed to me half of Utang had never been pumped by any check at all; it now taps through the way a person does. Proven by putting the bug back and watching the sweep name it: 'Utang (owed to me) at 1.0x shows a machine date: "Due 2026-08-15 - 1 entry" shows the stored date 2026-08-15'. That proof mattered more than usual here, because the check passed cleanly BEFORE the fixture and the tab were fixed, and a green check on an unreachable branch is exactly what the f2.84 retrospective was written about. A NOTE ON THE CHECK POLICING ITS AUTHOR: the first wording of this stamp was "says Aug 15 instead of 2026-08-15", and the new check failed the build on it, correctly. The Update stamp row IS user-facing copy and it contained a machine date. Reworded rather than exempted; an exemption would have been the first hole in a guard one minute old. NOT DONE, deliberately: no money math, no engine, no golden vector touched. |
| f2.91 | the journeys themselves, which ARE the QA pass in machine form, plus two deliberate breaks of real engine code | 0 defects found in the app; the finding is a whole class of coverage that did not exist | FOUNDER REQUEST: they are at work, cannot test everything by hand, and asked for test data and transactions that exercise features, plus an agent to own it. THE GAP, measured: 60 of the test files tap through a screen and every one drives ONE screen with a store built for it. Nothing tested a SEAM, meaning a defect that is correct on the screen that wrote it and wrong on the screen that reads it. That is not hypothetical, it is what three false alarms in one afternoon were all about: Home against Insights, Accounts against Utang, twice over a number that turned out to be fine. None could be settled by running a test, because no test had ever put two screens in front of the same store. WHAT SHIPPED: flutter/test/journeys_test.dart, six journeys that tap and type through the real screens in sequence and then check the money. The frame is INVARIANTS rather than expected values, and that choice is the load-bearing part. An expected value has to be recomputed by hand every time a fixture moves, and the moment that becomes tedious somebody pastes in whatever the code printed, which asserts what the code does and reads like proof. An invariant is a sentence that cannot be false: moving money between your own accounts cannot change net worth, paying a debt cannot either since an asset falls and a liability falls by the same amount, spending reduces it by exactly what was spent, lending and being repaid in full returns to the start, and whatever a write does in memory it does again after a reload from disk. EVERY INVARIANT IS PAIRED with a did-anything-happen assertion, because an invariant also holds when the action silently did nothing: a transfer that transfers nothing preserves net worth perfectly, and without the second half the test passes hardest when the feature is most broken. THREE OF SIX FAILED ON FIRST RUN and none of them were app bugs, which is worth recording because it is the same lesson as the render fixtures. Two were written from memory of where things used to be: Debts is not a Menu destination any more, it moved onto the Utang tab when the two were merged. One drove the transfer sheet by guessing its labels and tapped a widget sitting behind the modal. Fixed by reading the tests that already drive those flows instead of guessing, and the wrong assumptions are recorded in comments so the next author does not repeat them. PROVEN FAILING, twice, on real engine code rather than synthetics. One leg of a transfer multiplied by 0.9: "Expected: a numeric value within 0.001 of 15000.0, Actual: 15150.0", 150 pesos appearing out of nowhere, caught by name. A debt payment leaving the balance 1 peso high: "Expected within 0.001 of 7000.0, Actual: 7001.0". Both reverted; git diff on lib/ is empty. THE AGENT: .claude/agents/journey-tester.md owns this file and the discipline, including the five ways driving a screen wastes a round (a tap on an off-screen widget does not throw, it silently does nothing) and the rule that it never changes app code to make a test pass. CLAUDE.md gained the section. NOT DONE, deliberately: no pixels, since the render harness draws and the readability sweep measures; no money math or engine changed; and the journeys cover the write paths a person uses most, not every one, which is named here rather than implied by silence. |
| f2.92 | the lunch and learn, which audited the GUARDS rather than the app, plus hand verification of every claim it made before acting on any of them | 2 guards found HOLLOW and fixed, 1 dated fuse defused 2 days before it burned, 3 false claims in CLAUDE.md corrected, 2 screens added to the sweep; every fix proven failing first | THE RETROSPECTIVE FOR f2.86 THROUGH f2.91, run after the founder confirmed on the phone. It audited the guards instead of the app and that is where everything was. FINDING 1, and it is my own work from yesterday: two of the six journeys in f2.91 PASSED with the feature under test deleted. The transfer journey's companion assertion was bank plus cash equals 23000, which is exactly what a transfer preserves, sitting under a comment calling itself the honest half. Verified by removing the Move it tap so no transfer happened: All tests passed. Same for the utang round trip with both Mark paid taps removed. The two hollow ones were both journeys whose invariant is a CONSERVATION statement, changes nothing or returns to the start, and that is not bad luck: a conservation invariant is unfalsifiable by inaction by construction, so its companion can never be another conservation statement. The four healthy journeys assert a DIRECTIONAL change and carry their own proof for free. Fixed with per-account movement plus a stored-blob comparison, proven by deleting the taps again (Expected 1500, Actual 3000.0). CLAUDE.md now states the directional rule and why the earlier wording was not enough. TWO NON-BUGS LEARNED WHILE FIXING IT: the transfer runs Cash to Bank, not Bank to Cash, because the sheet defaults its source to the first account, and a conservation check could never have shown which way the money went; and recording an utang does not move cash unless a lending account is chosen, because cashLeg is true only then, since "I lent this last month, let me write it down" must not invent a withdrawal today. Both settled by reading receivables.dart rather than by touching the app. FINDING 2, a dated fuse two days from burning: the lived-in fixture pinned every date to a constant July 2026 while every screen compares against DateTime.now(). On 1 August every expense falls into last month, Budget reads "PHP0 of PHP18,000, nothing spent yet this month", and every screen reverts to the first-run empty state, silently, by the calendar. That is session 17's entire lesson returning on a timer. The receivable added in f2.90 to make the due-date branch reachable was the literal 2026-08-15, so that branch had a seventeen day fuse of its own. The stated reason for pinning, that golden images changing with the calendar are noise, protected a comparison that cannot happen: test/shots is gitignored, git ls-files returns nothing for it, and CI only ever runs the harness with --update-goldens, which writes and never compares. Anchored to DateTime.now() the way the app's own sample data already was, plus test/fixture_still_lived_in_test.dart asserting the states the fixture exists to present, proven by pinning to the previous month and watching it name the failure. FINDING 3: two of the four screens carrying f2.88's rounding fix, Reports and Debts, had never been drawn OR measured. Added to the sweep; both pass. FINDING 4, three false claims in CLAUDE.md, two of which I wrote yesterday: "pumps every screen" covered ten of fifty files, "asserts INVARIANTS, never expected values" was false with four literals already in the file, and "sixty other test files" was seventy-nine, sitting two paragraphs from the rule that says numbers in prose rot. All three corrected, and the sweep's screen list is now named as a list rather than described as a rule. NOT DONE, and named rather than implied: the real fix for the list-versus-rule pattern is deriving each guard's set from the codebase the way palette_contrast_test.dart already does with one line, expect(seen, barakoThemes.length * 2). That model was written in this repo the same week and was not reused; it is Open 7. The edit-pattern hook is Open 8 and is the founder's call, not an agent's. No money math, no engine, no golden vector touched. |
| f2.93 | five widget tests driving the real Menu card, plus a deliberate break of the seed to prove the data-loss guard, plus looking at both rendered states | 0 defects; 1 thing the founder asked for that had been quietly not delivered, now delivered; 1 known-bad edit pattern used AGAIN, for the tenth time | FOUNDER-VISIBLE and SIGNIFICANT, because it is a new write path on stored data. The founder asked for test data and transactions to poke at on their phone. What f2.91 shipped was tests that run on a runner, which they cannot see or touch, and nobody said out loud that the request had been read a different way. The retrospective caught that. The app has HAD a sample data generator all along, reachable from exactly one place, the last step of onboarding, so anybody already using Salapify could never get to it. Menu now offers it, with the way back in the same card. WHY IT CANNOT COST ANYTHING, and this is a property of the seed rather than a promise in a comment: every seeded row carries the sample_ id prefix, addSampleData APPENDS and reads nothing existing, and the pre-existing removeSampleData drops exactly that prefix plus rows that reference a sample fixture. No new data-loss surface was created: removal was already reachable from the Home banner, and this adds a second door to the same code. Idempotent, because tapping twice would otherwise create a second sample card and a second Jollibee, double every figure in between, and then one Remove would clear both so the doubling would never be visible afterwards. DELIBERATELY NO CONFIRM DIALOG on the way in, unlike Start fresh, and deliberately not warning-coloured. The two are not the same kind of action, and a scary dialog on a reversible one teaches people to dismiss the dialogs that matter. The card sits ABOVE Start fresh so somebody hunting for "let me try this" reaches the reversible one first; verified in the render, where the neutral outline against Start fresh's red reads as two clearly different weights of action. GUARD, proven failing first: five tests drive the real card, and the one that matters asserts every row the person typed is byte-identical after loading. Proven by making the seed replace instead of append and watching it fire: Expected '{"id":"mine","name":"My... Actual '###', which is every real row gone. There is also a round-trip test (load then remove lands back exactly where it started), an idempotence test, and a read-only test asserting the button is DISABLED rather than hidden, since a control that vanishes reads as a bug while a greyed one reads as a state. MY OWN PROCESS FAILURE, recorded because the session before last called it unguardable and the last one proved it is not: the python assert-before-write heredoc silently discarded an edit AGAIN, tenth outing, one turn after I described it as Open 8. The write never happened because a later assertion in the same script threw. Redone with the Edit tool. This is now the strongest argument in the repo for the PreToolUse hook, and it is still the founder's call. NOT DONE: no money math, no engine, no golden vector, no migration, and no change to how removal works. |
| f2.94 | four tests on the gate itself, two deliberate breaks, and a wrong version of my own check caught by its own proof | 0 app defects; 1 promise turned into a mechanism; 1 check of mine found too weak DURING its own proof and strengthened | FOUNDER REQUEST, made minutes after f2.93 merged: the sample data is for testing only and must be gone before the app store. Correct call, and the wrong way to honour it is a note in a chat log, because the gap between now and launch is months and that is exactly where a promise dies. So it is a mechanism: lib/build_flags.dart holds one compile-time constant, kPreviewBuild, read via bool.fromEnvironment('SALAPIFY_PREVIEW'), with kTestingAids named separately because the two will diverge (a preview build is also where a half-finished real feature lives, which is a different question from scaffolding). The Menu card is wrapped in if (kTestingAids). The store build passes --dart-define=SALAPIFY_PREVIEW=false and the code is not compiled in at all. Nothing needs editing at launch, which is the point: an edit can be forgotten, a build command is written down once. THE DEFAULT DIRECTION IS A DECISION, stated rather than assumed: it defaults to TRUE, so a forgotten dart-define ships scaffolding rather than silently STRIPPING a feature from the founder's preview. The second failure reads to them as the app losing something and costs a round to diagnose; the first is caught by test/preview_only_test.dart and by the launch audit, both of which run before a store build exists. MY OWN CHECK WAS WRONG TWICE, and both were caught by insisting on the proof rather than by review. First version of the label list included "Remove sample data", which flagged two innocent files: that string is also the Home banner's way out, it is pre-existing, and it must survive into a store build because somebody who chose the sample data during onboarding needs the exit whether this scaffolding shipped or not. Second, and worse: the source scan asked whether the word kTestingAids appeared anywhere in the file, and it PASSED with the gate deleted, because the word was still sitting in a comment two lines above explaining the gate. A check a comment can satisfy is the exact shape this project keeps relearning, narrower than its own name and green for the wrong reason. It strips comment lines and requires the code form if (kTestingAids) now, proven by deleting the gate and watching it name the file and all three labels. THE OTHER HALF IS PROVEN TOO: a widget test asserts the card IS present in a preview build, because a gate that hid it from the founder as well would be worse than no gate, they asked for it and they are the only person who can test by hand. A separate test asserts the flag is still read from the environment and still spelled SALAPIFY_PREVIEW, since replacing it with a plain true would silently break the release command while every test and every preview looked identical. NOT DONE, and asked rather than guessed: whether "remove this test data" also means the onboarding option to explore sample data, which is a shipped first-run feature affecting what a new user sees. That is a product decision with two readings and the retrospective an hour ago named saying-which-reading as the guard. No money math, no engine, no golden vector, no migration. |
| f2.95 | self review; a comment and a document, with no behaviour to test | 0 defects; 1 deferred decision given a home that will actually be opened | The founder was asked whether "remove the test data" also covers the ONBOARDING offer to explore sample data, which is a shipped first-run feature and a different thing from the testing card f2.94 gated. They chose to defer it, so this parks it rather than deciding it for them. docs/launch-checklist.md holds the decision with both cases argued, what to do for either answer, and the one part that is NOT up for debate: the Home banner's "Remove sample data" must survive into a store build whatever is decided, because a build that strips the exit while leaving the entrance is the worst of the options. WHERE IT IS PARKED IS THE WHOLE POINT. A decision cannot have a test written for it, so the only useful guard is a location somebody is certain to open at the right moment. flutter/lib/build_flags.dart now points at the checklist, and that file is what a person opens to set --dart-define=SALAPIFY_PREVIEW=false for a store build. The question therefore surfaces at the exact moment it must be answered, rather than in a document whose existence has to be remembered. NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGED, so there is nothing new to test and no guard is claimed; the existing preview_only_test.dart still covers the gate itself. Recorded as SKIPPED-equivalent honestly: this row exists because the stamp requires one, and the only verification available was reading the two files back. |
| f2.96 | the expanded sweep itself, which found both defects on its first run, plus a deliberate new screen file to prove the coverage check | 2 real user-facing defects found and fixed; the sweep went from 12 screens to 24; the coverage gap is now a machine rather than a paragraph | Open 7 from this morning's retrospective, the one named as the real lesson of the day: every guard here is a list wearing the title of a rule. The readability sweep covered TEN typed screen names against fifty files in lib/screens, and CLAUDE.md described it as covering every screen. Closed the way palette_contrast_test.dart already did it, which is the model that existed in this repo the same week and was not reused: enumerate what is on disk, then refuse to pass until every file is either swept or exempted WITH A STATED REASON. An exemption list is not a weaker form of coverage, it is what makes a gap visible; a new file in lib/screens now reddens the build until somebody decides which side it belongs on, and that decision is precisely what nobody was being asked to make. Proven by dropping a new screen file in and watching it named. IT PAID FOR ITSELF ON THE FIRST RUN, with two real defects on screens nothing had ever measured. DEFECT 1, and it is the third outing of the same class: Goals printed "PHP21,500 of PHP60,000 - by 2026-12-31", a stored machine date in a sentence. Insights had it (f2.84), the utang list had it (f2.90), and Goals kept it through both rounds of fixing it elsewhere because Goals was not in the swept set. Fixed with prettyMonthYear, which drops the DAY deliberately rather than showing it: a savings goal is aimed at a month, and "by Dec 31" invites somebody to read a deadline into an estimate. DEFECT 2: the Search hint read "Search anything, like jollibee, Ana, or 1500" and was CUT OFF at default font size on a 390pt phone, so the one example that teaches you a person's name is searchable was the part that vanished. Shortened to fit. TWELVE SCREENS ADDED and all twelve otherwise clean: Goals, Recurring, Search, Cash flow, Paluwagan, Tools, Treats, Ask Pan, Payday, Notes, Mindset, Privacy receipt. WHAT IS STILL NOT COVERED, named in the exemption map rather than implied by silence, because that is the whole point of the mechanism: five modal sheets, which is the biggest remaining hole; seven calculators, whose interesting states all need typing so a cold pump measures an empty form, which is a reason to DRIVE them and not to skip them; onboarding, correctly rendered against the opposite fixture; and the share screens, which need a chosen month or milestone. Each reason is short enough that a reader can disagree with it. NOT DONE: no money math, no engine, no golden vector. |
| f2.97 | self review of the eight touched trust-route flows, plus every new test proven to fail against the old behaviour before the fix | 8 route and truth defects fixed; 0 new defects; each fix proven with a test that reddens on the old code; no money math, schema, or golden vector touched | THE BATCH: eight places where a control did not do what it said or landed somewhere without the thing the user asked for. Empty Home "See who owes me" switched to the Utang tab on its DEFAULT "I owe" segment, a screen with none of the user's receivables; it now opens "Owed to me" through the shell callback. "Pay off a debt" PUSHED a standalone Debts screen over the shell, stranding the user with no bottom bar; it now opens the "I owe" segment in the tab. Search HID accounts behind a note claiming the Accounts screen "is not ported yet" (it has been for a long time), so an account match silently vanished; accounts are now shown, and tapping one opens Accounts scrolled to and flashing the matched row, with a deleted-before-tap id handled by a calm "that account was just removed" note rather than a crash. The Accounts "Manage debts on the Debts screen" copy now routes to the canonical "I owe" tab. The Insights empty CTA "Start logging" only switched to Home; it now opens the Log sheet directly. Onboarding budget stopped fabricating 20000: blank means set later, invalid shows an inline error that blocks Next, zero is an explicit no-budget choice, and a value past the 100,000,000 maximum is capped WITH the cap disclosed; both "set later" and zero resolve to the 0 the app already reads as "no limit", so no stored shape changed. Pan CTAs moved off a raw-string switch onto a typed PanRoute registry with an exhaustive switch; /accounts, /reports, /goals and /(tabs)/more used to fall through to no button and now open their screens, and a source-scan contract test fails on any responder route with no destination. Onboarding's finish error stopped showing the raw exception and now says what stayed safe and what to do next. PROVEN FAILING FIRST, every one: the Home CTA tests could not find "Money owed to you, oldest first" / "Owed to me" on the old code; the Search tests found 0 "ACCOUNTS"; the Insights test found 0 LogSheet; the onboarding tests found no error text and stored 20000; the Pan contract test named /accounts as unrouted. All reverts restored via Edit, git diff on lib clean afterwards. VERIFICATION LIMIT, stated rather than implied: this is a self review on a runner, not a hand test on the founder's phone; flutter analyze is clean and the full suite is green (1273 passing before these two policy rows), and the readability sweep plus the render harness were run for the touched screens. NOT DONE, deliberately: no money math, no engine, no golden vector, no migration, no React Native, no new tab or calculator. |
| f2.98 | self review; Open 11 investigated with a with-and-without-fix test comparison before writing anything | 0 defects; 1 "bug" proven NOT to reproduce and closed; 1 stale code comment corrected; 1 guard test added; no runtime behaviour change | THE ASK was to fix Open 11 from the f2.97 retrospective: the account focus-scroll was said to silently no-op for a match far below the fold on a long list, because a lazy ListView leaves the row unbuilt so Scrollable.ensureVisible finds no context. FIRST I checked whether it reproduces instead of trusting the finding, because the finding was a code-reading inference and this project has shipped tests written from the same wrong mental model as the code before. It does NOT reproduce. Every AccountStore.accounts row renders inside ONE eager Column, the "Cash and e-wallets" section anchored at the top of the list, and a Column builds all its children, so the row's element exists even when painted well below the fold; ensureVisible finds it and scrolls it in. PROVEN by writing accounts_focus_scroll_test.dart, which seeds 40 accounts and asserts the 40th (about 2800px down, viewport 600) has its rect ON the screen after the reveal, then running it against the code WITH a speculative scroll loop and WITHOUT it: both pass. So the scroll loop was speculative complexity for a non-bug and was dropped; the simpler original ensureVisible stays. The in-code comment that claimed the scroll did not work for long lists was therefore false and was corrected to say why it does (eager Column), with a pointer that the guard reddens if the rows ever become a lazy per-row builder. NET CHANGE: a new guard test plus a corrected comment plus this stamp; the compiled app is byte-identical, so nothing changes on the phone, and the stamp says exactly that. NOT DONE, deliberately: no money math, no engine, no schema, no golden vector, and no scroll loop, because a fix for a bug that does not exist is not a fix. |
| f2.99 | self review; PR A of the durable-store phase (ADR 0001), reviewed for zero behaviour change against the full suite plus new failure-injection tests | 0 defects; 1 seam introduced (LedgerRepository) with the store routed through it; the two data-safety invariants proven to survive the boundary; no stored-data, money, backup-format, or schema change | THE STEP: ADR 0001 was approved ("go with your recommendations"), and this is PR A, the smallest safe first move: introduce a LedgerRepository interface so the store and UI no longer touch SharedPreferences directly, and the encrypted durable engine can slot in later without changing a caller. The two ledger keys and the interface moved to lib/data/ledger_repository.dart (storageKey re-exported from store.dart so the ~40 tests that import it are unchanged); the default SharedPrefsLedgerRepository does exactly the reads and writes the store did inline, so nothing about what is stored, where, or in what format changed. The store's five persistence methods (load, _save, importBackupText, hasPreviousImportCopy, undoLastImport, startFresh) now call the boundary; startFresh still clears the FX cache and log directly because those are a separate subsystem. WHY IT IS SAFE, proven not asserted: the entire existing suite passes through the new boundary (1280 tests, the 1276 that existed plus 4 new), which is the zero-behaviour-change proof, because every persistence-reopen and golden and backup test exercises the default SharedPreferences engine unchanged. On top of that, ledger_repository_test.dart injects a fake engine and proves the two load-bearing invariants survive the seam: an unreadable ledger (readLedger throws) sets loadError, disables writes via canWrite, and is never overwritten; and a failed write (writeLedger throws) rolls back memory and never advances the durable state, so a failed write can never produce a success receipt. A second store on the same fake reads back what the first wrote, proving the store really uses the injected engine for both directions. NOT DONE, deliberately and per the ADR's PR split: no encryption, no Keystore, no native change, no SQLCipher, no migration, no money-representation change, no prefs split yet, no Data Health yet. Those are PR B (native, separately gated) and later PRs. This PR is pure Dart, ships over the air, and is reversible. |
| f3.01 | pre-merge GATE ran: qa-tester and principal-engineer agents reviewed the durable store, found two data-loss paths, both REPRODUCED with a scratch test before any redesign; then the design was reworked to remove them and re-reviewed | 2 critical data-loss findings caught by the gate and designed out (not patched); 0 remaining; 1 crash-safe file store added as a validated SHADOW, never authoritative in B1; two findings kept as regression tests, both proven to fail against the old behaviour | THE GATE DID ITS JOB, and this row is mostly about that. The first cut of PR B1 made the new file store AUTHORITATIVE and dual-wrote to SharedPreferences as a revert mirror. Two review agents run as a real pre-merge gate (the founder's standing instruction to use the agents as a gate) independently found the same root flaw: two independently-writable stores with no reliable which-is-newer signal. FINDING 1, silent loss: a file that was valid but OLDER than SharedPreferences (after a code rollback, or a session where the file store failed to open and only SharedPreferences advanced) was trusted over the newer copy, then overwrote it. FINDING 2, resurrection: a start-fresh interrupted partway left the file cleared and SharedPreferences intact, which the next launch misread as a first run and used to bring the just-erased ledger BACK. Both were REPRODUCED in a throwaway test first, per prove-it-before-you-fix-it, so neither was a code-reading guess. THE FIX WAS A REDESIGN, NOT A PATCH: for an over-the-air step, do not hand-roll a which-is-newer signal, avoid needing one. SharedPreferences stays the single source of truth and every read comes from it, exactly as before; the atomic file store is now a SHADOW, written alongside and kept in step but NEVER read back as truth in B1. A stale or half-cleared shadow therefore cannot override or resurrect anything, and a plain revert is lossless UNCONDITIONALLY because source was always the truth. The file store is still built, exercised and continuously synced so PR B2 (native SQLCipher, a transactional database that owns which-is-newer correctly) can promote it to authority with the data already present. Both findings are kept as regression tests and PROVEN to fail against the old shadow-wins behaviour (Finding 1: the newer shadow won; Finding 2: the erased ledger came back), then restored. Also folded in the reviewers' cheap notes: main() ensures bindings first, and the file store's comment no longer overclaims power-loss durability it cannot deliver. VERIFICATION LIMIT: self review on a runner, not a hand test on the phone; flutter analyze clean, full suite green, 22 durable-store tests. NOT DONE, deliberately: no encryption, no Keystore, no native change, no SQLCipher, no authority swap (that is B2), no money-representation change, no prefs split, no Data Health UI yet (the coordinator exposes shadowInSync and lastShadowWriteOk for a later surfacing). This PR is pure Dart, ships over the air, and changes nothing about which bytes the app trusts. |
| f3.02 | pre-merge agent gate (security-privacy-auditor, qa-tester, principal-engineer) on the encrypted store; the native engine cannot run on the runner so its safety logic was lifted into a pure-Dart coordinator and proven there; the gate found FOUR must-fixes, all fixed and re-proven | encrypted-at-rest store added (SQLCipher plus Android Keystore); 4 must-fix data-safety/privacy defects caught by the gate and fixed; 0 remaining; SIGNIFICANT and NATIVE, needs a base APK and a manual install | THE STEP: PR B2, the real encryption, the riskiest change in the phase. SQLCipher (sqflite_sqlcipher) holds the ledger JSON in an encrypted SQLite database; the key is a random 256-bit value kept in the Android Keystore via flutter_secure_storage, any run can read it so a sensor reset never costs data (App Lock stays the separate UI gate, ADR 0001). Native, so it CANNOT ship over the air: pubspec bumped 0.6.3+12 to 0.7.0+13, which the preview turns into mode=release, a fresh base APK the founder installs by hand. THE DESIGN: encrypted store authoritative; the old plaintext (B1 SharedPreferences plus the file) kept as a FROZEN read-only fallback, not deleted, per the founder's choice, so a rare Keystore key-loss shows the last safe copy rather than an empty or locked ledger; a later confirm-then-delete change removes the plaintext once the founder confirms on the phone. THE GATE EARNED ITS KEEP, four must-fixes, all fixed and re-proven: (1) SECURITY, the manifest had no allowBackup=false, so the still-plaintext ledger and the secure-storage prefs were eligible for Android Auto Backup to Google Drive, contradicting encrypted-at-rest; fixed with android:allowBackup=false. (2) DATA-LOSS, a stale B1 undo snapshot leaked through the frozen fallback so a healthy user could be offered, and tap, a revert to pre-upgrade data; fixed by scoping readUndoSnapshot to the encrypted era only, never the fallback, proven by a new test. (3) DATA-LOSS, if the encrypted store became unopenable later the app silently served the stale plaintext WHILE WRITABLE, losing post-migration writes and flip-flopping; fixed by making the store READ-ONLY (canWrite false) whenever it serves the fallback because encrypted is unavailable, and by building the coordinator over an unavailable-encrypted stand-in (not a writable plaintext store) when the database exists but will not open, proven by a store-level test. (4) DATA-LOSS, clearLedger cleared encrypted THEN fallback, so an encrypted-clear failure left plaintext full and the next launch migrated it back (erase-then-resurrect); fixed by clearing the fallback FIRST, and the existing SAFETY test could not catch it because its mock never threw on clear, so a new test makes the clear throw. UNTESTABLE NATIVE, handled: SQLCipher and the Keystore cannot run on the headless runner, so all the migrate-vs-read-vs-fallback logic lives in the pure-Dart EncryptedStoreCoordinator and is proven with fakes; the native engine is a thin key-value adapter. PROVEN BY DELIBERATE BREAK: the erase-order guard failed against the wrong order (fallback stayed full), then restored; the earlier fallback-clear break was also proven. BUILD RISK largely closed: the branch check runs flutter build apk --debug when the diff touches android/ or pubspec, so the Android build (SQLCipher AAR, manifest merge, compileSdk 36, minSdk 24 which clears both plugins) compiles BEFORE merge; that step must be green before merge, analyze+test is not enough for this PR. VISIBLE, not trusted: a Storage row on the update card reads Encrypted or Plaintext (fallback) so the founder can SEE which engine engaged. VERIFICATION LIMIT, loud: analyze clean and full suite green on the runner, but the actual encryption and Keystore only prove out on the founder's phone after the manual APK install; mode=release, so nothing reaches the phone until installed by hand. NOT DONE, deliberately: plaintext NOT deleted yet (confirm-then-delete follow-up), no biometric binding, no backup-format change, no money-representation change. |
| f3.03 | self review; the confirm-then-delete cleanup, pure Dart and OTA, gated so it can only run after encryption survives a relaunch; proven by deliberate break | plaintext safety copy now retired automatically once encryption is confirmed on a reopen; 0 defects; completes PR B2 | THE STEP: the final piece of the durable-encrypted-store phase, retiring the old plaintext copy that f3.02 deliberately kept as a safety net. The founder confirmed f3.02 on the phone (Storage: Encrypted, moved this run), which is the agreed signal to proceed. HOW IT IS SAFE: the retirement is SELF-GATED on a relaunch. The coordinator only deletes the plaintext fallback on a STEADY-STATE encrypted read, the branch that runs when the encrypted store opens on its own and already holds the ledger, which by construction never happens on the migration launch and never when the encrypted store is unavailable (that path serves the fallback and returns before reaching the retirement). So the safety copy cannot be removed until encryption has proven it reopens across a full app restart on the real device, and if the encrypted store ever fails to open the fallback is exactly what is kept. Best-effort and once-per-session: a failed delete leaves the copy in place and the next steady-state read retries.
