PROVEN BY DELIBERATE BREAK: adding the retirement call to the MIGRATION branch made the migration-run-keeps-the-fallback and first-run-migration tests fail (the fallback was cleared on the migration run), then restored. Three new assertions: a steady-state read retires the redundant plaintext, the migration run keeps it and a later relaunch retires it, and an unopenable encrypted store never retires it. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure Dart, no version bump (pubspec stays 0.7.0+13), so it is a Shorebird patch on top of the f3.02 base APK, no new install. VERIFICATION LIMIT: analyze clean and full suite green on the runner; the actual deletion runs on the founder's phone on the next reopen after this patch lands, and the Storage row will read plain Encrypted (no moved this run) once the copy is gone. NOT DONE: nothing further in this phase; money math, backup format, and the money-representation adapters (a later, separate effort) are untouched. |
| f3.04 | self review; a single guard test plus the session 21 retrospective, no runtime change | 1 machine guard added closing Open 13 (the highest-stakes finding of the encryption arc); proven by deliberate break | THE STEP: turn the session 21 retrospective's Open 13 into a machine in the same session it was raised. The pre-merge gate's strongest single finding on the encrypted store was that nothing asserted android:allowBackup=false, so a future manifest edit could silently re-enable Android Auto Backup and make the whole plaintext-and-prefs ledger eligible for Google Drive, with every other check still green. widget_manifest_test.dart already reads AndroidManifest.xml off disk, so the guard is one expect: the manifest must contain android:allowBackup=false, with a reason explaining the leak it prevents. PROVEN BY DELIBERATE BREAK: flipped the manifest to allowBackup=true, watched the new test redden (the ledger-cannot-leave-the-phone test failed), then restored to false and confirmed green. No runtime code changed (a test plus the stamp plus docs), so nothing on the phone changes; the stamp bump exists only because any push touching flutter/ needs a fresh stamp for the publisher. Also writes docs/lunch-and-learn.md session 21, the blameless retrospective for the whole B2 encryption arc, anchored on the founder's phone confirmations (f3.02 Encrypted moved this run, f3.03 yes it works). NOT DONE: no money math, no engine change, no schema, no golden vector. |
| f3.05 | pre-merge agent gate (security-privacy-auditor, qa-tester) on Phase 1 PR1, the Android privacy contract; both lenses ran on the full diff; the security lens found ONE must-fix and the QA lens found one should-fix, both fixed and re-checked before merge | Android privacy contract hardened: backups fully excluded (11 and 12+), FLAG_SECURE tied to App Lock, lock-screen reminders generic by default with opt-in detail, targetSdk pinned to 36, merged-manifest allowlist in CI, actions SHA-pinned; 1 must-fix + 1 should-fix fixed, 0 remaining; SIGNIFICANT and NATIVE, needs a base APK and a manual install | THE STEP: Phase 1 PR1, the founder-approved privacy/release/evidence phase, stage one (base APK first). Delivers, each with a guard proven to fail then restored: (1) complete backup exclusion, allowBackup=false plus res/xml/backup_rules.xml (Android 11 fullBackupContent) and res/xml/data_extraction_rules.xml (Android 12+ dataExtractionRules, BOTH cloud-backup and device-transfer, because allowBackup=false does NOT stop D2D transfer on 12+), every domain excluded, guarded by backup_posture_test.dart. (2) native secure-window, FLAG_SECURE set when store.appLockOn via a thin MainActivity.kt method channel; all the deciding logic is in pure-Dart secure_window.dart, tested with a mock channel. (3) generic lock-screen notifications by default, names/amounts moved out of titles entirely and into the body only when the opt-in settings.notifDetailed is on; detailed uses a VISIBILITY_SECRET channel (kept off the lock screen regardless of the user setting, shown in the shade after unlock), generic uses PRIVATE; the old test that ENFORCED the peso amount on the lock-screen line is inverted. (4) explicit targetSdk=36 (was the moving flutter default) with tests including the no-exact-alarm posture. (5) merged-manifest allowlist, check-merged-manifest.sh reads the MERGED manifest from a real build and holds permissions/exported/backup/cleartext to typed allowlists, wired into the branch check after the native APK build, its logic driven on the branch by merged_manifest_guard_test.dart. (6) all 8 actions SHA-pinned. (7) Flutter check on every PR via a pull_request trigger with a shared concurrency group so no duplicate runs. THE GATE EARNED ITS KEEP: security must-fix F1, the permission allowlist grep only inspected the android.permission.* namespace so an OEM/custom permission a dependency merges in slipped through; fixed to compare the full android:name value, proven by a new com.sec.android...badge.WRITE test case. QA should-fix, the copy and design said detailed detail is visible only after unlock, but VISIBILITY_PRIVATE only redacts when the user has separately chosen to hide sensitive content, so on show-everything phones it would leak; fixed by using VISIBILITY_SECRET on its own channel (channel visibility is fixed at creation, so a separate id is required), and the copy corrected. Also fixed opportunistically: a raw ISO date in one detailed reminder body (now formatted), a stale targetSdk comment, and the secure-window apply now commits its cached state only after the native call succeeds so a transient failure self-heals. VERIFICATION LIMIT, loud: analyze clean and full suite green on the runner, but FLAG_SECURE, the encrypted-DB backup exclusion, and the SECRET lock-screen behavior only prove out on the founder's phone after the manual APK install; mode=release, so nothing reaches the phone until installed by hand. NOT DONE, deliberately: this is PR1 only; the production flavor + AAB (PR2) and the evidence work, privacy-receipt copy, local metrics, README/pubspec descriptions (PR3), are separate PRs. No money math, schema, or golden-vector change. |
| f3.06 | pre-merge agent gate (security-privacy-auditor, play-launch-auditor) on Phase 1 PR2, the production build identity; both lenses ran on the full diff; the security lens found 0 must-fix, the launch lens found ONE hard delivery-blocking FAIL, fixed and guarded before merge | Production build identity split into separate preview and prod Gradle flavors so a store build cannot borrow the preview key, the "Preview" copy, or the testing aids; 1 delivery FAIL (the publisher was flavored but shorebird.yaml had no flavors map) fixed and guarded; 0 remaining; SIGNIFICANT and NATIVE, needs a base APK and a manual install | THE STEP: Phase 1 PR2, production release identity, the founder-approved decision that the Play build must never be confusable with the founder's preview build. Delivers: (1) two Gradle flavors on one applicationId, preview (committed preview key, "Salapify Preview" label, testing aids on) and prod (upload key from a CI secret only, "Salapify" label, SALAPIFY_PREVIEW=false so aids tree-shake out); buildTypes.release sets NO signingConfig so the flavor decides, the one shape that stops prod inheriting the preview key. (2) the upload key is env-only with no fallback and never committed; only preview-keystore.jks is in the repo, asserted by production_identity_test.dart which also proves prod->upload, preview->preview, release-has-no-signingConfig, and the label/title wiring. (3) a manual-only production AAB workflow (flutter-prod-aab.yml) that fails loudly if any of the four SALAPIFY_UPLOAD_* secrets is missing, builds the prod appbundle, then verify-prod-aab.sh refuses any bundle that borrows the preview identity: preview certificate (SHA-256 compare), "Salapify Preview" label, a manifest outside the permission/backup allowlist, or surviving sample-data strings in libapp.so. (4) the preview publisher now names --flavor preview on both shorebird release and patch, and its APK paths move to app-preview-release.apk. THE GATE EARNED ITS KEEP: the security lens cleared the signing/secret/backup posture with no must-fix (preview and prod cleanly separated at config, build, and artifact layers; backup exclusion, FLAG_SECURE, and generic-notification default untouched), and folded in one cheap improvement (the prod preflight checks all four upload secrets, not just the keystore, so a missing password gives the checklist message not a raw Gradle error). The launch lens found the hard FAIL: wiring --flavor preview into the shorebird commands without adding a flavors: map to shorebird.yaml would have made `shorebird release android --flavor preview` unable to resolve an app_id on the next merge to main, breaking the founder's ONLY live delivery channel, and it would have broken INVISIBLY because the branch check never runs a real shorebird release, only the merge does. FIXED: shorebird.yaml maps preview to the existing app_id (release history carries over); prod is intentionally absent because the production AAB is a plain flutter build appbundle, not a shorebird release, so production has no OTA by design (ADR 0002). GUARDED so the class cannot recur: flutter-check.yml asserts every flavor the publisher passes to a shorebird command has a flavors: entry in shorebird.yaml, PROVEN by deliberate break (with no flavors map the isolated block is empty and it reports "missing: preview"; with the map it passes). Also caught in wiring and fixed before the gate: the flavored release APK is app-preview-release.apk, not app-release.apk, so the verify-shipped check and the base-APK upload both moved to the flavored path, or a release run would have published to Shorebird server-side while the founder's release page got no installable APK. Operational notes carried into launch-checklist.md: the Play build cannot install over the preview build (different signing cert, wipes data, back up first), versionCode must strictly increase across prod uploads, production has no OTA on purpose, minSdk is still the Flutter default. VERIFICATION LIMIT, loud: analyze clean and the full suite plus the flavored debug APK build green on the runner, but the shorebird+flavor release/patch matching is only fully provable at the merge-to-main delivery (the branch check installs shorebird but never runs a real release), and the production AAB path has never executed because the upload secrets are deliberately unset until the founder submits to Play; both fail safe. pubspec bumped 0.8.0+14 to 0.9.0+15, so mode=release, a fresh base APK the founder installs by hand. NOT DONE, deliberately: PR3 (privacy-receipt copy from verified behavior, local metrics store plus tester view plus opt-in safe sharing, real README/pubspec descriptions) is separate and ships over the air. No money math, schema, or golden-vector change. |
| f3.07 | self review; the session 23 retrospective plus one durable delivery guard, CI and docs only, no app-logic change; the guard's selection logic proven against a mock asset list before trusting it | 1 open lesson from f3.06 turned into a machine (stale release-asset cleanup); retrospective written; 0 defects | THE STEP: close the one open lesson from the f3.06 retrospective (session 23) in the same session it was raised. On f3.06 the flavored build renamed the base APK from app-release.apk to app-preview-release.apk, which orphaned the OLD f3.05 asset next to the new one on the fixed flutter-preview release page, same size, so the founder had to send a screenshot asking WHICH to install; tapping the stale one would have silently installed an old build while everything looked done. THE GUARD: flutter-preview.yml gains a step that, after a successful publish, deletes every .apk asset on the release whose name is not the current base APK (app-preview-release.apk), so the page only ever offers one installable file. It runs on every successful publish (patch and release) so a stale asset is cleaned on the next push rather than lingering until the next base APK; this very f3.07 patch run is its first exercise and removes the current orphan. continue-on-error so a cleanup hiccup can NEVER block or false-alarm a real delivery: the APK is already published and the delivery row already written by the time it runs, so the worst a failure does is leave a stale asset, no worse than before the step existed. EXPECTED_APK is hard-coded to match the upload path a few lines above in the same file, with a comment that a future rename must change both together. PROVEN BEFORE TRUSTING: the shell selection logic was run against a mock asset list (app-release.apk plus app-preview-release.apk) and correctly picked the stale one to delete and kept the current one, and picked nothing to delete in the single-asset case. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure CI and docs, no flutter/ logic change; the pubspec stays 0.9.0+15, so this is a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no new install. The stamp bump exists because any push touching flutter/ (here, the one-line updateStamp) needs a fresh stamp for the publisher, and the patch run is also what triggers the cleanup. VERIFICATION LIMIT: analyze clean and full suite green on the runner; the cleanup step itself is a workflow step, so its live behavior only proves out on this merge to main, where it should remove app-release.apk and leave app-preview-release.apk. KNOWN OPEN, named not hidden: nothing yet asserts the verify-shipped path and the upload path in flutter-preview.yml stay EQUAL to each other (lesson 2's honest limit); carried forward. No money math, schema, backup format, or golden-vector change. |
| f3.08 | pre-merge agent gate (security-privacy-auditor) on Phase 1 PR3 part 1, truthful descriptions; the lens verified every new receipt claim against the code that implements it | Privacy receipt expanded to state the on-device protections it can prove (backups off, secure screen when App Lock on, generic lock-screen reminders); README and pubspec description rewritten from Flutter defaults; guard test added and proven; SIGNIFICANT (privacy-facing copy) but OTA, no base APK | THE STEP: Phase 1 PR3 part 1, make the app's own descriptions match verified behavior, the "product evidence" half of the phase. The receipt's existing network claims (two connections, no third) stay literally true because these are on-device protections, not new connections. WHY IT IS SAFE: no store, money, schema, backup format, or engine change; the receipt is display-only. The gate's job here was accuracy, not security surface: it checked that each new sentence is true against the code, since a privacy receipt that overclaims is worse than none. The privacy-safe counts and the review-gated manual copy ALREADY existed (Diagnostics.report, surfaced behind the Update card), so this does not rebuild them; it surfaces the same safe report as its own always-on screen (lib/screens/diagnostics_screen.dart) reachable from Menu > Privacy, the founder's chosen "local view". WHAT IT SHOWS: only Diagnostics.counts(store.data), which reads list lengths (ints) under seven hard-coded generic keys and can never carry a label, plus the trimmed error buffer (developer exception strings, not user data). THE SHARE IS USER-INITIATED: Copy (clipboard) and Share (share_plus OS share sheet) fire only on a button tap; there is no automatic upload, fetch, or socket in the diff, so the Privacy receipt's "two connections, no third" stays literally true (a clipboard write and an OS share-sheet handoff are user actions, not app connections). Diagnostics gained one public accessor, counts(data), exactly as safe as the report line it mirrors (ints only, no new field). | f3.10 | pre-merge agent gate (flutter-ux-craftsman) on the seven-issue UI/accessibility/visual-regression change set; the lens read every changed screen and widget and checked large-text layout, reachability, tax wording preservation, and golden determinism | Seven UI/a11y/testing issues fixed; 0 must-fix, 2 should-fix + 2 polish all applied; tax wording confirmed byte-identical; OTA, no base APK | THE STEP: a focused engineering brief covering seven issues, all pure Dart plus test-only PNG baselines (no pubspec asset, no base APK). (A) The theme-mode "System" label broke at large text: the shared Segmented control now reserves the check-glyph slot on every segment (symmetric wrapping) and adaptively stacks when a label cannot fit on two lines, proven clip-free at 1.0/1.3/1.5/2.0x in segmented_test.dart (fail-then-restore). (B) The transfer-sheet actions could scroll out of reach: the fields scroll in a Flexible and Cancel/Move-it are pinned below as a Wrap (the Row overflowed at 1.5x); tests for constrained height, long names, scroll-to-activate, and the double-tap guard. (C) Insights hierarchy: a single-line WHAT MATTERS NOW summary (shown only when there are decisions, never repeating a title), a genuine error state on store.loadError (new shared error_state.dart), and the empty state migrated to the shared EmptyState; error-state test proven by deliberate break. (D) The Income tax screen: hand-rolled labels replaced with the shared Kicker plus header semantics, and the graduated line-item breakdown folded behind a "Show the calculation" expander; EVERY BIR string, form number, deadline, rate, and disclaimer is byte-identical (verified by diff) and nothing compliance-related is hidden (totals, set-aside, forms with deadlines, and the disclaimer all stay visible); phtax engine untouched. (E) Debug banner: production already had it off, but the harnesses did not, so it painted into every shot; a shared golden_app.dart (banner off, animations off, en/LTR) plus the flag on all 27 screens_shot MaterialApps. (F) A deterministic golden suite (test/golden/ui_golden.dart, named without _test so it never gates a plain flutter test) with committed baselines for the touched screens/states (system selector, transfer normal + constrained-large, insights empty + error, currency offline, tax-income, bir-dates, year-end-tax, shared empty + error), a scoped 0.5% tolerance comparator, a README, and a NON-BLOCKING CI compare step (the founder's "no flaky CI pixel gate" decision; the layout-metric tests remain the real gate). Generated and compared twice, identical. THE GATE: flutter-ux-craftsman found no must-fix; its two should-fix (Segmented fit-probe font, CI golden made non-blocking) and two polish (Insights summary to one line, transfer error capped) were all applied and re-verified. LOOKED AT: the transfer sheet (pinned actions, no banner), the currency-offline state, the Income tax screen (folded calc, deadlines visible), and the large-text selector were all rendered and reviewed. VERIFICATION: dart format clean on the changed files (the repo at large is not format-clean, pre-existing, not touched), flutter analyze zero, full suite 1384 green, golden compare stable across two runs. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure Dart, no pubspec version bump, a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no install. No money math, schema, backup format, or golden-vector change. |
| f3.11 | none, delivery-integrity fix, agent gate SKIPPED with reason | SKIPPED agent gate; stamp bump only, app behavior byte-identical to f3.10; no code change beyond the stamp string | THE STEP: a delivery-integrity fix, not a feature. The banked font-truth PR (#276) merged to main assuming a test-only change ships nothing over the air, which was wrong: the publisher runs on every push that touches flutter/, and Shorebird ships a patch even when the compiled app is functionally identical, so patch 5 went live under the UNCHANGED stamp f3.10 (the same stamp as patch 4). The publisher's one-stamp-one-build guard correctly failed its record step (STAMP == PREV == f3.10) and opened issue #277, leaving patch 5 live but unrecorded, the delivery log and the phone out of step. THE FIX: bump updateStamp to f3.11 so the new build carries a unique name; the publisher then writes the delivery-log row and #277 auto-closes on the green run. WHY NO AGENT GATE: there is no new app behavior to review. The only lib/ change since f3.10 is the stamp string itself; the font-truth test and doc diff already merged and was gated by flutter-ux-craftsman on f3.10. No money math, schema, backup format, or golden-vector change. VERIFICATION: flutter analyze zero, update_stamp_test and qa_record_test green, dart format clean on main.dart. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no install. |
| f3.12 | none, calendar-boundary test fix, agent gate SKIPPED with reason | SKIPPED agent gate; test fixtures only, no lib/ change beyond the stamp string; app behavior byte-identical to f3.11 | THE STEP: a calendar-boundary test fix, found on 2026-08-01 when the branch check went red on main with two failures unrelated to any code change. The lived-in fixture (test/screens_shot.dart livedInBlob) dated entries as "the nth of the current month, capped at today", which on the 1st and 2nd of a month collapses every date onto today: the weekday-spending pattern then has one active day and does not render (reports_screen_test "decision graphs render without overflow" failed), and no receivable is dated before today so the Overdue branch is unreachable (fixture_still_lived_in_test "somebody is overdue AND somebody still has time to pay" failed). reports_screen_test carried the same fixed-day-of-month bug in its own fixture. THE FIX: date entries RELATIVE to today. ago(k) is k days before today, so ago(0) is always in the current month and a spread of k lands on many weekdays across the last four weeks, crossing the month boundary as real recent spending does; receivables use ago() for overdue and ahead() for still-has-time; income is ago(0) so Reports and Insights always have this-month income (its absence otherwise surfaced the "No income logged yet" empty state, which overflows at 1.5x, a pre-existing latent layout bug FLAGGED for a later fix, not hidden). WHY NO AGENT GATE: no app behavior changed; the diff is test fixtures plus the stamp string, and the two originally-failing tests are the guards, already proven to fail (they failed today) and now pass. VERIFICATION: full suite 1385 green (was 1383 passed 2 failed), flutter analyze zero, both originally-failing tests confirmed green. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no install. No money math, schema, backup format, or golden-vector change. |
| f3.13 | pre-merge agent gates: flutter-ux-craftsman on the milestone celebration UX, and journey-tester on the directional journeys | 1 must-fix + 5 should-fix/polish, all applied and re-verified; 0 outstanding | THE STEP: the milestone celebration moment. The milestone engine (money/milestones.dart) and the branded share card (screens/milestone_share.dart) already existed and were tested, but nothing lit the loop at the instant of a win. Now, when a save crosses a real money win (a debt cleared to zero WITH logged payment pesos, a goal funded to target, an utang settled), the app opens a sheet offering the branded card to share (showMilestoneCelebration), then fires the existing confetti as a send-off. Transition flags fire once at the crossing, so NO stored-data change and no re-nagging; a debt zeroed by hand with no payment pesos is excluded by the engine (paid > 0) and falls back to plain confetti. Milestone gained a stable `id` and a `milestoneFor(data, id)` lookup; store.addGoalFunds returns the goal id only when funding just crossed the target. WIRED: debts.dart, utang.dart (both collect-payment and Mark-paid), goals.dart. THE GATES: flutter-ux-craftsman found one must-fix (confetti fired BEFORE the modal, so its scrim buried the burst) fixed by firing confetti after the sheet closes and announcing the win to a screen reader on open; five should-fix/polish all applied (dedup the capture/share pipeline so it truly lives once, a heading semantic on 'You just made it', a full-size off-screen capture so the shared PNG is crisp on every phone width, a drag handle, and a fully tappable hide-amounts row with its subtitle); the payable (utangOut) live-celebration asymmetry is documented in the engine as a deliberate follow-up. journey-tester added four directional journeys, each proven fail-then-pass (a debt to zero celebrates once and a partial payment does not; funding a goal past target celebrates once and re-funding does not; collecting an utang in full celebrates; a hand-zeroed debt shows no sheet), and every screen still agrees on net worth after each. BUNDLED HARDENING (session 26 follow-ups): screen_readability_test now measures the PAINTED right edge (both corners via localToGlobal) so a FittedBox-scaled headline is not falsely flagged (proven by a fail-then-fix break; the 'No income logged yet' overflow was this false positive, not a real bug), and a weekday-spread data-level guard in fixture_still_lived_in_test. LOOKED AT: the celebration sheet rendered in dark (shots/milestone-celebration-dark.png) and reviewed, reads well, no overflow, orange icons and Pan resolve. VERIFICATION: flutter analyze zero, full suite green (1389 with the new tests), dart format clean on the changed files. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure Dart, no pubspec version bump, a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no install. SIGNIFICANT (monetization/virality surface and a store return-value change), reported to the founder after merge. No money math, schema, backup format, or golden-vector change. |
| f3.14 | pre-merge design and QA gates: behavior-scientist on the comeback cadence (timing, copy, double-ping), and qa-tester on the built feature | 0 must-fix; 1 should-fix (utang-only users silently skipped by the data gate) fixed with tests; 3 polish accepted as consistent with backup; alarm proven both halves fail-then-pass | THE STEP: the notification comeback cadence (roadmap R23). Every other reminder is armed relative to the last app open and the schedule is only rebuilt on open, so a user who stops opening Salapify eventually runs dry and nothing brings them back. A new pure `comeback` kind in money/reminders.dart arms a gentle re-engagement ladder relative to now: with the daily nudge OFF it fires at now+2, +4, +7, +14 days at 11:00; with daily ON it fires ONLY the day 14 catch (daily already sends a 20:00 nudge every evening for 14 days, so the early pings would double-ping). SILENT FOR ACTIVE USERS BY CONSTRUCTION: the service (services/notifications.dart) wipes and rebuilds the whole schedule on every open, so each open cancels the old day 2 ping and re-arms it two days past the new open; only a genuinely lapsed user ever lets one fire. Gated on a shared _hasAnyData (accounts, transactions, debts, OR receivables non-empty) so a first-run bounce gets nothing. Messages carry no name, amount, or date, so they are lock-screen safe in both generic and detailed mode. Defaults ON with the nightly nudge at onboarding (rides along with daily:true, store.completeOnboarding) and is toggleable in Menu (new 'Come back' row). THE DESIGN GATE: behavior-scientist repositioned the roadmap's rough 1,2,3,7 sketch to 2,4,7,14 (day 1 reads as clingy and drives opt-outs; day 14 is the last catch before the permanent-silence cliff), set the daily-gate to kill double-ping days, kept 11:00 (an empty slot between the 9 to 10am and 20:00 kinds), and wrote the four escalating-warmth, guilt-free copy lines used verbatim. THE QA GATE: qa-tester found 0 must-fix (no crash, data-loss, or privacy leak; static strings, junk-data safe, decline path yields no comeback) and one should-fix: the data gate (copied from backup) checked only accounts and transactions, so an utang-only user, the core audience, was silently skipped by BOTH comeback and the monthly backup nudge; FIXED by widening to the shared _hasAnyData helper (adds debts and receivables) used by both kinds, with new tests locking in comeback-for-receivables-only and backup-for-debts-only. Three polish notes accepted as consistent with the existing backup nudge (the lone day 14 catch is the cap-sacrificial item, now noted in a comment; sample-only and onboarding-force behaviors match backup). ALARM RULE, both halves proven fail-then-pass: silencing the comeback branch reddened the FIRES test while the two SILENT tests correctly stayed green, and removing the daily-gate reddened the "daily ON fires ONLY day 14" test while FIRES stayed green; both restored after the runs reported. LOOKED AT: the Menu notifications card rendered in dark with the new toggle on (shots/menu-notifications-dark.png) and surfaced to the founder. VERIFICATION: flutter analyze zero, full suite green, dart format clean on the changed files. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure Dart plus a test PNG, no pubspec version bump, a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no install. SIGNIFICANT (notifications scheduling, and it widens the existing backup nudge to utang-only users), reported to the founder after merge. No money math, schema, backup format, or golden-vector change. |
| f3.15 | pre-merge gates, three lenses: qa-tester on the engine and edge cases, flutter-ux-craftsman on the screens, journey-tester on the write path | qa-tester: 3 must-fix (1 real engine bug) all fixed with proof, 6 should-fix applied; ux: 4 must-fix + 10 should-fix/polish applied, 3 accepted with reasons; journeys: 3 added, 0 real bugs | THE STEP: the Sweldo Timeline, Breakthrough 1 from docs/Product_Vision_Spec.md. A new pure engine (money/timeline.dart) projects liquid cash day by day ACROSS month boundaries: every recurring occurrence per month (the posted-month rule generalized so a paid bill is never double counted), every debt bank cycle, payday markers from the schedule only, a variable-spend band (the last 28 days of ordinary logged expenses, recurring posts and debt payments and sample rows excluded, divided by a fixed 28), and pure what-if overlays (a big buy, an extra monthly payment, more income, a spending cut). Twenty-one hand-computed vectors in test/timeline_test.dart, the multi-month claim proven by deliberate break. The Cash flow screen grows horizon chips (This month and To payday free; 30/60/90 days Pro with a working unlock door, free during early access), the band and hollow payday dots and a backed dip label on the chart, a WHAT IF card (saved to settings.timelineScenarios, on/off switches, add/edit sheet, inert and locked when Pro is off per the categories precedent), honest footnotes (band labeled an estimate, assumptions named, receivables never income), and an empty-window line. Home gains THE ROAD AHEAD glance card (free horizon sparkline, tightest day named, PressableScale, hidden when nothing is projectable). One new reminder kind, lookahead: a single heads up at 18:00 the evening before the conservative projection first dips below zero in 14 days; conservative line only, never the band, so the alarm cannot cry wolf on an estimate; both halves proven by deliberate breaks (silencing reddened FIRES with SILENT green; unconditional fire reddened exactly SILENT-on-healthy); generic body carries no amount, name, or date; Menu toggle off by default. THE GATES EARNED THEIR KEEP. qa-tester found a REAL engine bug: the debt-cycle cursor broke out of the loop whenever a due date was weekend or holiday adjusted (bankDueDate keeps the previous raw due in the running while its adjusted date is ahead, so the cursor saw the same date twice and the seen-set break discarded every later cycle), which understated debt outflow on roughly two of every seven due days; FIXED by stepping past the adjusted date and continuing, proven fail-first with a weekend-due vector (dueDay 18, Jul 18 2026 is a Saturday). Also from that lens, all applied: sheet junk-shape guards (backup preserves scenarios verbatim; is-checks and an orElse so a restored junk scenario cannot crash the editor), an Infinity/1e999 amount guard with a 100 million cap (tryParse accepts both and jsonEncode then threw AFTER the sheet closed showing success), run-out copy unified on firstNegativeDate so the card, the Home caption, the chart semantics, and the reminder all name the SAME day, the lived-in fixture's recurring rows stamped lastPosted so store.load cannot mutate fixture content by the real calendar day (the session 26 rot class), the Pro horizon clamped when Pro turns off with the screen on the stack, the scenario list re-read after the sheet await, income-before-outgo ordering within a day, payday guard raised for weekly schedules, freeHorizonDays rolling to the next payday or next month end instead of a zero-day window, and an "after this view" hint on out-of-window purchases. flutter-ux-craftsman must-fixes, all applied: the Pro snackbar pointed at a Menu unlock that does not exist, replaced with an Unlock free action that flips Pro and applies the user's intent (tested); the what-if sheet scrolls so large text plus keyboard cannot overflow; the stale-date picker crash clamped; the MergeSemantics that swallowed one of the row's two actions split so screen readers get both. Its should-fixes applied (inert-when-locked rows, edit glyph, hollow payday dots readable in all sixteen palettes, empty-state line, PressableScale, haptic only on success, real plurals, dated chart semantics, dip-label backing, remove receipt); accepted with reasons: independently scaling figures at extreme numbers, horizon resets per visit (a settings write, banked), duplicated date helper (predates this change). journey-tester added three journeys, each with directional companions and proven failure lines: a saved what if changes NO real money (net worth to the centavo, accounts/transactions/debts/receivables/goals byte-identical) while timelineScenarios grows and survives reload; the NOW figure equals the liquid sum, never the savings-inflated total; the switch lifts and restores the overlay without touching money. It found zero real bugs. LOOKED AT: the Pro 60-day view rendered dark (shots/cashflow-timeline-dark.png) and reviewed twice; the first render caught the shot capturing the wrong view (an off-screen chip tap that silently missed) and two grey boxes that were the dip label drawn in the test-default Ahem font because the raw TextPainter named no fontFamily, which also meant the phone drew it in Roboto instead of Jakarta; both fixed and re-rendered. VERIFICATION: flutter analyze zero, full suite green with the new vectors and journeys, dart format clean on changed files. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure Dart plus test PNGs, no pubspec bump, a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no install. SIGNIFICANT (money math: a new projection engine feeding a reminder, a Home card, and a Pro surface), reported to the founder after merge. No schema or backup format change; timelineScenarios rides the settings passthrough. |
| f3.16 | pre-merge gates, three lenses: qa-tester on the plan engine and data traps, flutter-ux-craftsman on the plan card and sheet, journey-tester on cross-screen plan journeys | qa-tester: 2 must-fix (1 permanent data trap, 1 dead deadline branch) + 6 should-fix, all fixed; ux: 3 must-fix + 4 should-fix + 5 polish applied, pinned-card follow-up named; journeys: 2 added, 1 REAL routing bug found and fixed with proof | THE STEP: Pan With a Plan, Breakthrough 3 from docs/Product_Vision_Spec.md, first slice. Pan holds exactly ONE standing plan in settings.activePlan, written only when the user taps a "Make it a plan" chip under a debt-free or goal-pace answer. A new pure engine (money/plan.dart): activePlanOf is the sole shape-checker (junk from a restored backup reads as no plan everywhere), periodsCompleted is calendar-honest (monthly clamps day-of-month through short months, the edge the FIRST vectors exercise; weekly is whole weeks), planStatus measures progress from startLevel so hand adjustments count like payments (states done, orphaned, started, ahead, behind, onTrack, plus the card's clamped progress fraction, computed in the engine not the screen), and planOfferFor prefills the offer (highest-rate debt at the asked extra or its minimum; the resolver's OWN focus goal at the goalPace deadline pace). The chat gains a 'plan' intent (strong multi-word phrases, EMPTY any-list so no old routing is stolen, goldens replay green; plano folds to plan) answered by the same planLine the card shows. The OUR PLAN card sits above the chat: a facts line (amount, cadence, start date, the trust rule made visible), one plain status sentence, a progress bar, Change (amount-only sheet with inline validation), and Drop behind a one-line confirm (it erases startDate and startLevel, which no remake restores). THE GATES EARNED THEIR KEEP, ALL THREE. qa-tester found a PERMANENT DATA TRAP: a near-complete goal produced a "Make it a plan: P0 monthly" chip (the greater-than-zero check ran before rounding), and accepting stored a plan activePlanOf rejects, so no card, no Drop button, and the raw-getter offer guard then blocked every future offer, forever, surviving backup export; FIXED by rounding before the bound check, capping at the sheet's own 100 million, and switching the render guard to activePlanOf (proven fail-first: the disabled guard reddened exactly the zero-offer vector, amount 0.0 stored), with a widget test that a junk stored plan never blocks new offers. Its second must-fix: the goal offer read a dueDate field goals never store, so the deadline branch was DEAD CODE and the chip could contradict the reply above it (the test fixture shared the wrong field name, passing for the wrong reason); FIXED by deriving from goalPace over targetDate including month-only values, with the fixture corrected and month-only and behind-goal vectors added. Also applied: offers re-anchor startDate and startLevel at TAP time so a chip left in chat history cannot backdate the commitment (and a deleted target gets an honest no-write receipt), clearActivePlan copies settings instead of mutating the rollback snapshot through a cast view, all three write paths catch store failures with a visible receipt, and an absurd asked amount falls back to the minimum payment. flutter-ux-craftsman: the card broke the feature's OWN trust rule (amount, cadence, start date invisible in every ongoing state), fixed with the always-visible facts line and a save receipt that echoes the new amount; the sheet's validation SnackBar drew behind the modal barrier so Save looked dead, fixed with inline errorText (the sheet became a real StatefulWidget so its controller outlives the exit animation); Drop had no confirmation for an unrecoverable erase, fixed with the Keep it / Drop it dialog (tested both ways). Should-fixes applied: every receipt path scrolls into view, the chip label derives its cadence word from the offer and wraps at 320dp with the target NAMED on the button, engine-owned progress, decimal-honest prefill, Change hidden on done and orphaned plans, cardKicker token, progress-bar semantics label. Named follow-up, not silently dropped: pinning a compact card above the chat (the full card would eat a third of a 320dp screen). journey-tester wrote two cross-screen journeys (make a plan in Pan, pay the debt on the Debts screen, ask Pan again: the plan's actual rises by exactly the debt's movement and the reply matches the card; drop the plan: money byte-identical, net worth to the centavo, the no-plan answer returns) and FOUND A REAL BUG shipped in this very change set: with a standing plan, "how is my plan" got the fallback reply, because the resolver spread planStatus last and its kind (debt/goal) overwrote the 'plan' discriminator; the single-screen ask test had passed on the CARD's text alone. FIXED by spreading first and pinning kind after (planKind carries the plan's own kind), and the widget test now demands planLine appear TWICE, card and reply. Prove-fail lines recorded for the zero-offer guard, both journeys, and the clamp vector. LOOKED AT: the plan card rendered dark twice (shots/pan-plan-dark.png), before and after the facts line landed; the shot itself asserts OUR PLAN is in frame before capturing, per the session 28 lesson. VERIFICATION: flutter analyze zero, full suite green including the new vectors, widget tests, and journeys, dart format clean on changed files. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure Dart, no pubspec bump, a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no install. SIGNIFICANT (money-adjacent: a standing commitment derived from debt and goal math, stored in settings), reported to the founder after merge. No schema or backup format change; activePlan rides the settings passthrough. |
| f3.17 | pre-merge gates, three lenses: qa-tester on the goal engine, store writes and junk backups, flutter-ux-craftsman on all three screens, journey-tester on cross-screen goal journeys; every must fix re-checked against the merging commit because the tree moved during the pass | qa-tester: 4 must-fix (1 committed money-destruction break, 2 junk-backup crashes, 1 empty-chips crash) + 4 should-fix, all fixed and re-verified; ux: 5 must-fix + 11 should-fix applied, 3 accepted with reasons; journeys: 2 added, both proven fail-first | THE STEP: the Goals redesign (founder spec) plus the app-wide icon system. ICONS FIRST: lib/widgets/salapify_icon.dart is now the single authority for every authored icon. 206 raw Icons.* constants across 51 files, 131 distinct names, four different checked glyphs and 13 authored emoji all collapsed into the meaning map, with size tokens, one filled/outlined selection pair, a distinct multi-select checkbox pair (a check-all list drawn as radio circles told users to pick one), and tooltips or semantic labels on every icon-only control including the previously unlabelled delete-debt and delete-note buttons. USER emoji are untouched everywhere, per the standing rule. test/icon_system_test.dart holds the perimeter with two derived scans (no raw Icons.*, no authored emoji outside a reasoned user-data allowlist), proven fail-first with a planted probe file that both scans reported line-exactly. GOALS: a pure engine (money/goal_plan.dart, 34 edge-first vectors) computes deterministic status labels (Completed, Paused, Overdue, Needs adjustment, Ahead, On track) from an explainable linear pace, required contributions matching goalPace to the peso, capped what-if projections with month-length clamping (Jan 31 plus a month is Feb 28, caught by QA as a skip to Mar 3), quarter milestones whose 100 crossing defers to the milestone sheet (proven fail-first), data-grounded templates (the emergency fund suggests three months of readable essentials or honestly nothing; pasko aims at December 1; debt payoff exists only when a live debt does), a safe-to-set-aside estimate on safeToSpend minus a user buffer that is null without anchoring data, and debt-payoff goals that derive live from their linked debt with target stored ZERO so every legacy consumer ignores them (search gained an explicit skip; it was rendering 0 of 0). Store: contribution history on every add and transfer, patchGoal, reorderGoals, restoreGoalRow for full-fidelity delete-undo, transferGoalFunds that clamps, records both sides, cancels wholesale when the destination vanished mid-flight (proven fail-first: guard disabled reddened exactly that vector with money destroyed) and leaves no fingerprints on a no-op; goals joined ensureEntityIds so duplicated ids from merged backups cannot multiply credits or swallow rows on delete. Screens: the plan-first list (summary, FOCUS suggestion labeled as one, accessible reorder, collapsible Paused and Completed), the template create flow with a real date picker, explained suggestions, semantic icon grid with spoken labels, token accents never selected by color alone, and a one-line plan review; the detail with quarter row (a Wrap, it overflowed 320dp as a Row), THE PLAN card with the expandable estimate, Add money one tap from the list card, what-if defaulting to the plan's own pace with the year always printed, dated history with an honest truncation count, and MORE holding pause, move-money (validated against the source so the receipt can never claim unmoved money) and delete behind a confirm whose Undo restores the exact row. A REGRESSION the gate caught: the redesign had quietly lost the old screen's downward saved correction, stranding a typo as Completed forever; the Adjust sheet now edits saved in both directions. A goal check-in reminder kind (monthly, two goals, generic titles, off by default) proven both halves. THE GATES EARNED THEIR KEEP AGAIN, and one finding is a process lesson: qa-tester found the COMMITTED tree carried a transfer that destroyed ten percent of every peso moved, because a milestone commit was made while the journey-tester's deliberate prove-fail break was live in the shared tree; the fix was already in the working tree (the agent's restore) and the new journey is the regression gate, but the near-miss goes to the next retro by name. Junk-backup crashes (a string contributions field red-screening the detail and breaking every fund and transfer; a numeric accent red-screening the whole list) were proven by probe and fixed with is-checks at every site. ACCEPTED WITH REASONS: sheet double-tap pop (navigation annoyance, money applies once), pinned compact plan card and bottom-pinned Save (named follow-ups), the 12-row history cap now says what it hides. LOOKED AT: three dark renders (list, detail, empty), each asserting its view before capture, re-rendered after every layout fix. VERIFICATION: flutter analyze zero, full suite 1516 green including 17 journeys, dart format clean. SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure Dart, no pubspec bump, a Shorebird patch on the f3.06 base APK, no install. SIGNIFICANT (money-adjacent goal engine, store write paths, and an all-screen icon migration), reported to the founder after merge. No schema or backup format change; every new goal field rides the sanitize spread. |
| f3.18 | pre-implementation flutter-ux-craftsman typography audit (validated the semantic scale, flagged the TextTheme-default overflow risk), then the automated harness on the merging tree: flutter analyze, the full test suite, the screen_readability sweep at 1.0x and 1.5x, and the render harness; a diff-level review of the migrated call sites was partially run (session limit) and found the Text.rich base-color cases to be non-issues (child spans set their own color) | Pass, 0 must-fix. Renders preserved 1:1 by construction (exact size via copyWith when off-ladder, exact weight, color via tint, height/letterSpacing/tabular kept); the ONE intentional visual change is the synthetic w500 weight replaced by real medium (w600), which Jakarta actually ships. Left-as-is styles are the principled set (pdf export, off-screen share cards, monospace receipts, size-sensitive inputs, button-foreground-inheriting labels, user emoji), documented in the PR | THE STEP: a central typography system (lib/typography.dart) that mirrors the React Native app scale (mobile/theme.js: caption 12, small 13, body 15, subtitle 17, title 22, big 28, huge 34, display 42; weights 400/600/700/800, heavy reserved for money and titles) onto the shipped Plus Jakarta Sans face. TypeScale (16 named rungs), TypeWeight (four real-file weights, no w500), AppText semantic roles (title/heading/subtitle/body/label/small/caption/micro/kicker plus money roles amountHero/Xl/Lg/amount/amountRow with tabular figures), and terse .w4/.w6/.w7/.w8/.tint()/.tabular modifiers. Deliberately did NOT wire a size-changing Material TextTheme: the audit showed defaulting bare Text from 14 to 15 would silently enlarge ~147 inherited sites and risk 320dp overflow, so bare Text renders exactly as before and centralization comes only through explicit AppText at call sites. About 739 hardcoded TextStyle sites across 63 files migrated to semantic roles by nine parallel agents over disjoint batches, each analyze-clean; the ten synthetic w500 uses fixed to medium. New guard test/typography_test.dart pins the eight RN sizes, verifies every weight maps to a real Jakarta file, and scans all of lib for synthetic weights; proven fail-first (it listed all ten w500 sites before the migration removed them). SHIPS OVER THE AIR: pure Dart and one doc, no pubspec bump, a Shorebird patch. Presentation only, no money math, schema, or backup change. |

| f3.22 | qa-tester, then flutter-ux-craftsman, both scoped to the working-tree diff (`git diff origin/main -- flutter/lib flutter/test`), founder-authorized to use agents this session; screen rendered dark per the render rule (three states looked at directly, not just built) | qa-tester: 2 MUST FIX (both real bugs) + 2 SHOULD FIX + 1 nit, all fixed and re-verified; flutter-ux-craftsman: 1 SHOULD FIX + 3 polish + 1 copy nit, all applied | Two real bugs from qa-tester, both in the new Waiting-list write paths. FIRST: `_cancelWaitingItem` (Cancel and its Undo) called `Reminders.reschedule(widget.store.data, ...)` on the line right after `patchMindsetWaitingItem(...)`, with no `await` between them. `_mutate` queues writes on `_writes.then(...)`, and `.then()` always defers to a microtask even on an already-completed Future (confirmed with a small repro), so the reschedule read `widget.store.data` from BEFORE the status flip landed. Since `Reminders.reschedule` wipes and rebuilds the whole notification schedule from whatever data it is handed, Cancel silently failed to cancel the item's own "Still thinking it over?" push; it kept firing until some later, unrelated reschedule happened to catch up. Every sibling write path already awaited correctly; only Cancel/Undo skipped it, and also swallowed write failures silently. FIXED: both are now `async` and properly sequenced (patch, await; reschedule, await), with the same try/catch-and-snackbar pattern the other four actions use. SECOND: `_saveToWaiting` (the "Revisit in 24 hours" button) had no busy guard, unlike every comparable save action in this app (payday.dart, recurring.dart, paluwagan.dart, treats.dart all check/set a `_saving` flag). A fast double tap could fire it twice on identical answers, producing two separate waiting items with two independently scheduled reminders. FIXED with a `_savingToWaiting` flag checked at entry and disabling the button while set; a new test proves the visible outcome (one tap or two, only one item saved) though, said plainly, disabling the guard and rerunning that exact test still passed in this harness (tester.tap()'s own internal pump appears to let the mocked, near-instant store write finish between the two calls), so the test is basic regression coverage, not proven-failing evidence for the race itself. SHOULD FIX, both applied: "Yes, review again" and "No, skip it" could silently discard unrelated unsaved input (a different in-progress item being considered, or unsaved Small Wins text) if a second waiting item happened to be due at the same time; now asks "Replace what you have?" first, proven failing first (disabled the check, watched the new confirm-dialog test fail on "Found 0 widgets with text \"Replace what you have?\"", restored, reran green). The new `waiting` reminder kind had no per-kind cap, unlike goals' `activeDated.take(2)`; a heavy user of the pause feature could crowd out the shared 60-notification ceiling with near-term pings ahead of bills or utang reminders; capped to the ten soonest by revisit time, sorted before truncating so the nearest ones win. UX pass, all applied: the Waiting row's peso amount lacked `.tabular` (every other money-in-a-row spot in the app uses it) and had no overflow guard unlike the name line right above it, both fixed and confirmed clean on a render with a ₱3,499 item; "Yes, review again" repopulated the considering fields but never scrolled back to them (the WAITING card the person tapped from is below the fold), so the change was invisible except for a snackbar, fixed with a `ScrollController` that animates to the top after restoring; the not-due row's only signal was two subtle colour swaps, close in luminance in the light palette, strengthened with `Opacity(0.6)` on the whole row rather than colour alone; a 12dp gap before the Revisit button bumped to 16dp; Menu's new toggle row copy dropped a quoted idiom ("wait and see") that was the only one of its 8 siblings using one. Rendered dark and looked at directly, not just built: the Waiting section (one due row reading "Ready to revisit" in orange, one not-due reading "Revisit in 19h" dimmed, a ₱3,499 amount with no overflow), the Revisit in 24 hours button in its own pause24h card, and the Do you still want this? sheet (filled/outlined/text hierarchy reads clearly). Re-verified after every fix: `flutter analyze` 0 issues on the whole project, `dart format` clean, the full suite (1604 tests) green. | Money Mindset Phase 3: "Pause for 24 hours" gains a "Revisit in 24 hours" button, saving the paused purchase (item, amount, category reference, the original three answers and result, created and revisit times, status) to `settings.mindsetWaiting`, the same Flutter-only passthrough pattern `settings.activePlan` and `settings.timelineScenarios` already use (additive, no migration, survives backup export/import). A compact WAITING section lists items still waiting with a live remaining-time label and a Cancel action (patches status, never deletes, with Undo). Once due, tapping a row opens "Do you still want this?": Yes restores the item into the decision tool with the three answers cleared, No prefills Small Wins without auto-saving, Not sure extends the wait 24 hours. Nothing here ever creates a transaction or moves a balance. THE REVIEW, six findings, all adopted: (1) FIX NOW, real accessibility bug: `_SelectableRow` and the checklist row each wrapped an outer `Semantics(label:...)` around a descendant that rendered the identical string again as plain `Text` with nothing excluding it, so a screen reader announced everything twice in one merged utterance ("Option A. Option A. Button, not selected."); fixed by wrapping each row's content in `ExcludeSemantics`, the same pattern the comparison block's own per-criterion row already used, and, for the checklist row, folding the item's `required`/`explanation` text into the ONE outer label first so excluding the descendant never silently dropped the explanation from what a screen reader hears. (2) FIX NOW, usability: the comparison block's "Mark as reviewed" toggle reused the choose-one radio icon pair (`selected`/`unselected`) that scenario and myth/fact use for picking one of several alternatives, misrepresenting a single acknowledgment as a multiple-choice option; fixed by giving `_SelectableRow` an icon-pair parameter and passing the checklist's own `checked`/`unchecked` pair for this one call site, keeping the icon vocabulary one-to-one with meaning. (3) FIX NOW, retention: every other block showed an `InteractionFeedbackCard` the instant the learner committed, but the reflection block gave no visible sign anything had registered beyond a small "Reset" link appearing bottom-right; fixed with a short, low-key "Thanks, noted." confirmation announced via a live region the moment the reflection engages. (4) polish: `InteractionFeedbackCard`'s kicker text ignored its own computed accent colour, always rendering in the neutral muted `Barako.kickerStyle` even when the icon and border went caramel for a caution, reading as a missed style; fixed with `Barako.kickerStyle.copyWith(color: accent)`. (5) polish: after submitting a sorting attempt, the Move Up/Move Down controls disappeared entirely, so a learner who saw exactly one item out of place could only discard the whole attempt back to the scrambled start via Try again; fixed by keeping the move controls live whenever the submitted order is not yet fully correct, and clearing the stale submitted-feedback state on the next move so Submit re-checks the adjusted order instead of losing an otherwise-correct attempt. (6) note, re-implemented rather than applied literally: the agent's suggested fix (a debug-only `assert` in each block's constructor body) is not legal Dart, a `const` constructor cannot have a body at all, only an initializer list, which is exactly why the original `List.length` asserts were removed from the initializer lists during this phase's own build (Dart cannot const-evaluate `.length` there either); implemented the same intent instead via `isValid` getters on `ScenarioChoiceBlock`, `ComparisonBlock`, `ChecklistBlock`, and `SortingBlock` (the last also checking for duplicate item ids), mirroring the existing `KnowledgeCheck.isValid` convention in `lesson_model.dart`, with new tests proving each catches its malformed fixture. | f3.30 | qa-tester, the pass f3.28/f3.29 explicitly deferred, read `expansion_content_policy.dart`, `interaction_completion.dart`, `content/interaction_blocks.dart`, `widgets/interaction_block_views.dart`, and their test files, confirmed reachability by checking `learn.dart`/`lessons.dart` never reference any Phase 4/5 file and no core lesson sets a topic or interaction block | 4 findings, 2 must-fix (a crash and a silent completion-gate bypass) plus 1 real validator defect and 1 cosmetic, all four fixed and re-checked; each new regression test proved failing against the pre-fix code before being trusted | THE STEP: no new product surface, a full bugfix pass closing the QA gap PR #307's own description named ("Not ready to merge yet... no qa-tester pass has run on Phase 5 yet"). FINDING 1, MUST FIX, crash: none of the six interaction block widgets keyed themselves, so `viewForInteractionBlock` rebuilding the same tree position with a DIFFERENT block (reordering, filtering, swapping, exactly what a future expansion reader would do) let Flutter reuse the old State, leaving `_pickedId`/`_order` referencing an id absent from the new block; the first read (`ScenarioChoiceView`'s `firstWhere`, `SortingView`'s `_itemFor`) threw `Bad state: No element`. Fixed by keying every case in the dispatcher with `ValueKey(block.blockId)`, so a different block at the same position always gets a fresh State. FINDING 2, MUST FIX, silent completion-gate bypass: `blockId` is documented "unique by convention" but nothing checked it across a list, so two different REQUIRED blocks accidentally authored with the same id let completing one silently mark both complete, since `allRequiredInteractionsComplete` only ever checks a `Set<String>` of ids. Added `hasUniqueBlockIds`, a guard function mirroring `SortingBlock.isValid`'s own duplicate-id check, documented as a precondition on `allRequiredInteractionsComplete`; deliberately not a throw, matching this file's "never throw for a normal problem" convention, so a future reader validates once before trusting the gate. FINDING 3, validator defect: five of the seven hard-error regexes in `expansion_content_policy.dart` had no negation awareness and flagged the exact safe phrasing the policy exists to protect: "No provider can guarantee a return" (guaranteed-outcome), "This account is not risk-free" (same code), "You are not eligible... unless" and "not covered... until" (eligibility), "will never ask you to share your OTP" and "Never enter your password" (credential-request); separately, "Buy Now, Pay Later" and "A Buy Now Pay Later loan" (a real loansOrCredit topic name) tripped the buy/sell-a-named-asset pattern on the capitalized word "Now". Fixed the first five with `_isNegatedNearby`, a clause-scoped check (cut at the nearest comma/semicolon/period before the match, so an unrelated earlier clause's own "no" or "not" can never suppress a real violation later in the same sentence) that looks both just before a match and inside it, since the eligibility pattern's own `[^.]{0,20}` wildcard can capture a "not" between "you are" and "eligible"; fixed the sixth with a narrow `(?!Now\b)` lookahead on the buy/sell pattern, naming the one known exemption rather than a broad allowlist. A new regression test proves the fix does NOT create a false negative: "This fund has no annual fee, and guarantees a profit every month" still flags, because the comma cuts the clause before "no annual fee" reaches the "guarantees" match. FINDING 4, cosmetic: `_isPlaceholderHost`'s `h.contains('.example.')` was a raw substring check; tightened to a label-exact split so "example.gov.ph" is still caught the same way but nothing can match on a coincidental substring. WHAT WAS CHECKED BEFORE TRUSTING THE FIX: every existing positive test in `expansion_content_policy_test.dart` (This fund guarantees a profit every month, Buy Fund XYZ today, You are already eligible, Enter your OTP here, the reviewed-myth-example fixture) re-verified to still trip its hard error after the negation change, since none of them carries a negation word in the scoped clause. PROVEN FAIL-FIRST: all three code fixes were reverted together (the dispatcher key removed, `hasUniqueBlockIds` stubbed to always return true, the negation/Buy-Now patch reverted with `git apply -R`), the new tests rerun, and every one failed for the documented reason (`StateError:<Bad state: No element>` on both dispatcher-swap tests; `Expected: false / Actual: <true>` on all seven negation/Buy-Now false-positive tests and both `hasUniqueBlockIds` tests), then the fixes were restored from saved patches and the full suite rerun green before this row was written. VERIFICATION: `dart format` clean, `flutter analyze` 0 issues, 2 new dispatcher-swap widget tests, 4 new `hasUniqueBlockIds`/duplicate-id tests in a new `interaction_completion_test.dart` (the pure-function counterpart `interaction_completion_integration_test.dart` never had), 9 new negation/Buy-Now false-positive tests plus 1 no-false-negative test in `expansion_content_policy_test.dart`, full suite green at 1840 tests. NOT DONE: no new interaction block types, no expansion reader screen, no wiring into `learn.dart`; PR #307 (Phase 4 + 5) is mergeable from this row onward. |

| f3.37 | self review (a founder-requested content simplification of already-shipped, already-reviewed content; no new claim, no new topic, no new interaction); real test failures during the edit were the actual proof, not a separate agent pass | 1 real test failure caught and fixed mid-edit, self-caught by running the affected test after every batch of edits rather than at the end; 0 defects remaining | THE STEP: the founder read the shipped Grow Your Money courses and flagged that "fictional" was repeated far too often, most severely in the Deposits course's fund fact sheet lesson, which said "(fictional)" on nearly every single line (11 nuggets, 2 to 4 mentions each). Total count across the three courses' lesson files: 124 mentions. Rather than a blanket find-replace, first read `lessons_stocks_bonds_content_test.dart`'s and `lessons_deposits_pooled_funds_content_test.dart`'s own "every fictional company/fund is labeled fictional" tests to learn what is actually test-enforced: a LENIENT nearby-window check (80 characters before to 200 after, or 20 before to 60 after for the fact-sheet fund name) requiring "fictional" to appear once somewhere near a specific named example ("Example Snack Co.", "Example Balanced Growth Fund"), not a requirement that every sentence repeat the word. This meant the vast majority of the 124 mentions were pure authoring habit, not a safety requirement, and could be cut freely as long as the ONE instance next to each named example was preserved. Added `examplesAreFictional` (default false) to `EducationalBoundaryBlock` (`content/lesson_blocks.dart`) and a matching fixed extra sentence in `EducationalBoundaryView` (`widgets/lesson_block_views.dart`, rendered only when true): "Every company, person, and figure used as an example is invented for teaching, never a real one." Set `examplesAreFictional: true` on all three courses' `_boundary` constants (`lessons_grow.dart`, `lessons_stocks_bonds.dart`, `lessons_deposits_pooled_funds.dart`), the ONE disclaimer surface each lesson already carries exactly once, so this is genuinely one clear statement, not a new second disclaimer competing with the first. Then trimmed the lesson bodies: the fact-sheet NuggetsBlock (85 mentions in that one file alone) down to a single "(fictional)" tag on the one named fund; per-item labels in CategorizeBlock/ComparisonBlock exercises (where the block's own prompt or title already says "fictional" once) had the redundant per-item repeat dropped, e.g. "A fictional government bond" to "A government bond" once "Sort each fictional product into..." already framed the whole exercise; "Fictional user A/B/C/D" to "User A/B/C/D" once "Four fictional situations, side by side" already framed the comparison table those names appear in. THE ONE REAL BUG THIS CAUGHT: stripping "fictional" from a `CategorizeItemDef` label containing "Example Snack Co." in `lessons_stocks_bonds.dart` broke `lessons_stocks_bonds_content_test.dart`'s "every fictional company is labeled fictional" test, because the categorize prompt's own "fictional" mention sat outside that specific item's 80-before/200-after window once the item's own local mention was removed; caught immediately by rerunning the test after that edit (not deferred to the end), fixed by restoring "fictional" directly next to "Example Snack Co." in that one label, re-ran green. This is exactly why every batch of edits was test-verified before moving to the next file, rather than editing all three files and testing once at the end. WHAT WAS PRESERVED ON PURPOSE: the `(fictional)` tag beside every distinctly named example (Example Snack Co., Example Delivery Co., Example Balanced Growth Fund, Portfolio A/B/C) stayed exactly where it was, since these are the specific instances the compliance tests check and the specific instances a reader could otherwise mistake for a real, searchable name; one scene-setting "fictional" per scenario/block title also stayed (e.g. "A fictional trust officer...", "A fictional friend shares..."), since a single clear framing sentence per exercise is normal, readable writing, not the problem the founder flagged. VERIFICATION: after every file's edits, reran that file's own content test before moving on; `dart format` clean, `flutter analyze` 0 issues on the whole project; full `flutter test` suite green at 2021 tests, unchanged from before this edit (a content and one small model-field change, no test count change since no new interaction, block, or lesson was added); a new golden render (`screens_shot.dart`'s `deposits-read-a-fact-sheet`, the lesson that had the worst repetition) added and looked at directly, dark and light, shown to the founder in chat, confirming the fact sheet reads clean and the boundary card's new sentence renders correctly; a second, scratch-only render (written, run, viewed, then deleted, never committed) scrolled to the boundary card specifically to confirm the exact rendered sentence, since the default shot's fixed viewport does not reach that far down the lesson. NOT DONE: no new `philippine-policy-reviewer` or `investment-literacy-reviewer` pass for this change, since nothing here adds, removes, or alters a factual or regulatory claim; the change only reduces how many times an already-true statement ("this is invented") is repeated, and the one test-enforced instance per named example was deliberately kept untouched throughout. If a future course adds real new claims alongside a similar simplification, that combined change would still need its own review pass. |

CONFIRMED, NOT FINDINGS: the course's own choice to never use the acronyms VASP or CASP in reader-facing text is the SAFER choice, not a gap; WebSearch confirms BSP registers/supervises "Virtual Asset Service Providers (VASPs)" while SEC separately introduced a "Crypto-Asset Service Provider (CASP)" licensing framework in 2025, two distinct, agency-specific, currently-live terms rather than one superseding the other, so staying acronym-free avoids picking the wrong one for a given claim. The course's choice to never embed the BSP VASP directory PDF (link the live BSP Verifier and SEC's own site instead) is confirmed the safer design: WebSearch turned up multiple distinct historical versions of that same PDF path (dated across 2022 to 2026), meaning the file genuinely gets republished in place over time, exactly the staleness risk the course's own header comment named as the reason to avoid it. BSP Verifier's own description ("does not guarantee safety or assess financial soundness or risk") matches this course's `RiskWarningBlock` wording almost word for word. The consumer-warning taxonomy in Lesson 5 (guaranteed returns, fake giveaways, impersonation, phishing, romance and trust-building scams, recovery scams, pump-and-dump groups, pressure to act immediately, credential requests, fake support, recruitment schemes, screenshot-as-proof) matches the general pattern of red flags SEC Philippines investor-education material warns about. Fixed the same way: narrowed to assert only that this course's own contiguous run still sits where expected via `sublist`, with a comment pointing forward to `lessons_crypto_content_test.dart` for the full, now four-course assertion. VERIFICATION: `dart format` clean, `flutter analyze` 0 issues on the whole project, full `flutter test` suite green at 2103 tests (up from 2021), new focused tests across 4 new files (`lessons_crypto_content_test.dart`, `lessons_crypto_progress_backup_test.dart`, `loss_impact_and_risk_review_blocks_test.dart`, `portfolio_shock_illustration_test.dart`) covering registration and isolation from the core 22 and all three earlier courses (both directions), the Phase 4 validator, official-source and risk-warning and educational-boundary requirements, every course-specific interaction the task's own spec named per lesson, house rules (no em/en dash, no guaranteed-outcome language, no named real coin/token/platform/provider, no eligibility claim, no buy/sell instruction, no credential request), the exclusions list (no live price, no return forecast, no staking/mining/NFT mention, no purchase recommendation as the checklist outcome), Salapify-action route safety, the loss-impact arithmetic (30/60/100 percent plus a 0 percent and a never-assumes-growth invariant), and the risk-review checklist's exact three summary strings.
