| f3.39 | self review (flutter-ux-craftsman consulted for the design decision only, read-only, no code access; implementation and verification done directly, no qa-tester pass, a small UI-only change to one existing screen) | 1 real bug caught and fixed before shipping, by rendering and looking, not by reading the code | THE STEP: founder attached a screenshot of the Menu screen's Reminders card and asked to make it collapsible, since 9 reminder toggles (Log reminder, Payday, Bills due, Cash flow heads up, Money to collect, Monthly backup, Goal check-in, Paused purchase check-ins, Come back) plus a tenth detail toggle ("Show names and amounts"), each carrying a two-to-three line explanation, made it the longest scroll on the screen. Consulted flutter-ux-craftsman (read-only) for the collapse design before writing any code; it recommended per-row subtitle collapse over hiding whole reminders behind a "more" disclosure, specifically because CLAUDE.md's own "nothing a user already has moves behind a wall" rule rules out any design where an already-configured switch could be out of reach without an extra tap. Implemented as a new `_ReminderRow` StatefulWidget in `flutter/lib/screens/menu.dart`, replacing the old bare `row()` closure and the separate hand-written "Show names and amounts" row (both now built the same way): each row seeds its own `_expanded` state from the CURRENT switch value at build time, not always collapsed, so a reminder someone already turned on stays visible on the exact screen where they would come back to reconsider it. Tapping the title area (not the switch) toggles the subtitle open or shut; the switch is a separate sibling outside that tap target, never nested inside it, so flipping a reminder never requires expanding it first. The subtitle Text widget stays mounted at all times, collapsing only its rendered height (`AnimatedSize` around `ClipRect(Align(heightFactor: ...))`), specifically because the row is still wrapped in the same `MergeSemantics` a prior a11y sweep added to fix an unlabeled-switch gap; a conditional `if (_expanded)` build would have dropped the subtitle from the semantics tree exactly when collapsed, silently reopening that gap for a screen-reader user even though sighted users would see nothing wrong. Followed two existing precedents rather than inventing new ones: the icon swap and `ExcludeSemantics` wrapper on the chevron matches `_CollapsibleTool` in `insights.dart`; the general shape of a quiet in-card disclosure matches the `ExpansionTile` used in `tax_calculator.dart`. THE BUG: the first version used `Align(heightFactor: _expanded ? 1 : 0)` alone, without `ClipRect`. `Align.heightFactor` shrinks the widget's own LAYOUT box but does not clip its child's PAINT, so a collapsed row's subtitle kept drawing at full size while occupying zero layout space, visually overlapping the divider and the start of the next row underneath it. This was invisible reading the code and obvious in the first rendered screenshot (dark, lived-in fixture, a mix of on and off reminders on purpose so both states would show at once): every row appeared expanded regardless of its actual state, with dividers cutting through the middle of subtitle text. Fixed by wrapping the `Align` in `ClipRect`; re-rendered and confirmed clean in both dark and light, collapsed rows now correctly showing only title, icon, chevron, and switch. VERIFICATION: `dart format` clean, `flutter analyze` 0 issues, full `flutter test` suite green at 2103 tests (unchanged count, no new tests added since this is a pure UI change to already-covered code, not new logic), including `screen_readability_test.dart`'s existing "Menu lays out at 1.0x and 1.5x system font" checks (both passed, confirming no overflow at large text) and the existing `menu_grid_test.dart`, `sample_data_menu_test.dart`, `name_ask_test.dart`, and `preview_only_test.dart` widget tests that construct `MenuScreen` (none of them touch the reminders row's internal structure, so none needed changes). Two scratch-only renders (written, interacted with by tapping a collapsed row to confirm independent expand-vs-switch behavior, viewed directly in both themes, then deleted, never committed) stand in for a dedicated golden shot, since Menu is not a Money Courses screen and this is a small interaction change to existing chrome rather than new content. NOT DONE: no qa-tester agent pass (a UI-only change, not a money-math, storage, or security change); no new automated test added for the collapse/expand behavior itself, since nothing here is money logic or data shape, the existing screen-level layout and accessibility tests already cover this row, and the founder's own "look at the screen" discipline plus the caught-and-fixed clipping bug stand as this change's verification. |
| f3.40 | two flutter-ux-craftsman consultations (read-only, no code access; implementation and verification done directly, no qa-tester pass, a UI-only change to two existing screens) plus the app's own automated a11y sweep | 1 real accessibility bug caught and fixed by the a11y sweep itself, not by rendering or reading the code; 9 pre-existing tests updated to reach content that moved behind a collapse | THE STEP: the founder pushed back on f3.39's per-row Reminders fix twice, clarifying they wanted whole SECTIONS of Menu (Settings, Helpers, Reminders) collapsible, not just individual rows within one card, "since it is a bit overwhelming when everything is expanded," and asked for it rolled out app-wide with UI/UX agent input before implementation. First flutter-ux-craftsman consultation (scoped to Menu) pushed back on collapsing the MONEY and HELPERS nav grids at the tile level: they carry icon-plus-label only, no explanatory prose, so they never had the "wall of text" problem Reminders had, and hiding destinations behind a tap risks making features feel removed. A second, follow-up consultation (the founder's own request for a second opinion before implementing) answered the narrower, correct question, whole-SECTION collapse, and drew a real distinction from the first: hiding a whole grid behind a closed section still leaves its HEADER visible as a wayfinding cue, unlike hiding individual tiles, so the objection is real but weaker at this level. Landed on: MONEY stays fully untouched (still the single most-used section, no collapse at all, not even section-level); HELPERS gets a `CollapsibleSection` defaulted OPEN (still primary navigation, a first-time visitor should never open Menu and find Tools or Treats missing); PERSONALIZE, YOUR NAME, SECURITY, and REMINDERS each get their own `CollapsibleSection` defaulted CLOSED (set-once settings, not places someone navigates through daily, so collapsed-by-default is where the actual space saving comes from); PRIVACY and YOUR DATA left alone as out of scope this pass. Two new widgets landed in `flutter/lib/widgets/section.dart`, shared rather than screen-private: `CollapsibleSection` (a bare `Kicker` header collapsing an arbitrary child, for a whole page section) and `CollapsibleCard` (a boxed header collapsing one peer card among several, promoted out of `insights.dart`'s own private `_CollapsibleTool`, which until now only covered that screen's TOOLS band). Promoting it let a second target apply the exact same interaction rather than a hand-rolled copy: `insights.dart`'s "THE BIGGER PICTURE" band (5 always-open cards, spoken-for, money health, the 6-month trend, categories, emergency runway) was the top pick from a separate app-wide survey this session ran in parallel, specifically because that screen already had a working sibling precedent one section up and is the most-used analytics tab in the app. Wrapping those 5 cards required stripping each card function's own internal `Kicker` title line first (`_spokenForCard` twice, once per return branch, plus `_healthCard`, `_trendCard`, `_categoriesCard`, `_runwayCard`), since the title now lives once in the `CollapsibleCard` header above it; leaving the old internal Kicker in place would have shown the same title twice whenever a card was opened. THE REAL BUG: `CollapsibleSection`'s tap target used `BoxConstraints(minHeight: 44)`, copied verbatim from the second agent's own suggested code, 4 pixels under Android's real 48dp minimum tap target guideline. This was not caught by rendering and looking, the same discipline that caught f3.39's clipping bug; it was caught by `test/a11y_test.dart`'s own automated sweep (`Menu meets all four guidelines, every screenful`), which failed with the PERSONALIZE, YOUR NAME, REMINDERS, and SECURITY headers each measuring exactly `Size(350.0, 44.0)` against the required `Size(48.0, 48.0)`. Fixed by raising `minHeight` to 48, matching `CollapsibleCard`'s own already-correct value (itself unchanged, inherited from `_CollapsibleTool`'s original code). Left uncorrected, this would have shipped a genuinely too-small tap target on four Menu sections, the exact class of defect a look-alone pass cannot catch since it is a hitbox measurement, not a visual one; this is the argument for keeping the automated a11y sweep in the suite rather than treating "look at the screen" as sufficient on its own. NINE PRE-EXISTING TESTS FIXED, all because content that used to render unconditionally now sits behind a closed section by default: `name_ask_test.dart` (3, tap "YOUR NAME" before finding the Set/Remove/Change buttons), `insights_screen_test.dart` (3, the old Kicker strings 'MONEY HEALTH' and 'SPOKEN FOR EACH MONTH' no longer exist at all, replaced by the new CollapsibleCard header text "Your money health score" and "How much of your salary is spoken for", each tapped open before the inner content it used to check for is checked), `currency_test.dart` (1, tap "PERSONALIZE" before finding "Currency"), `mood_theme_test.dart` (1, same, before finding "Appearance"), `widget_privacy_test.dart` (1, tap "SECURITY" before finding the widget-privacy switch). VERIFICATION: `dart format` clean, `flutter analyze` 0 issues, full `flutter test` suite green at 2103 tests (unchanged count, no new tests added, a UI-only change to already-covered code and interaction paths). Rendered and looked at directly, dark and light: Menu's top (MONEY untouched, HELPERS open with its chevron), Menu scrolled to the collapsed PERSONALIZE/YOUR NAME/REMINDERS/SECURITY band (all four now a single line each, PRIVACY and YOUR DATA reached almost immediately after), REMINDERS expanded to confirm the new section-level collapse composes cleanly with f3.39's own row-level collapse inside it with no overlap, and Insights' Bigger Picture band collapsed to five single lines matching the sibling TOOLS band's look exactly. Three scratch-only renders (written, interacted with, viewed, then deleted, never committed). NOT DONE: no qa-tester agent pass (UI-only, no money math, storage, or security surface touched); Reports (needs a different fix, tabs rather than collapse, per the earlier app-wide survey) and Privacy receipt (lower priority, low-traffic screen) are deferred, reported to the founder as candidates rather than implemented in this pass. |
| f3.41 | one flutter-ux-craftsman consultation (read-only, no code access; implementation and verification done directly, no qa-tester pass, a token-level styling change to one widget) plus palette_contrast_test.dart's own automated sweep across all 16 palettes | 0 new defects; the founder's own visual feedback was the actual finding this batch, not a bug an agent or test caught first | THE STEP: founder attached a screenshot of f3.40's shipped collapsed sections and said the closed rows (YOUR NAME, REMINDERS, SECURITY) were "not appealing," specifically that nothing signaled there was more content to reveal: a closed `CollapsibleSection` header was a bare `Kicker` plus a small `Barako.muted` chevron, visually IDENTICAL to a plain, non-interactive label like PRIVACY or YOUR DATA, which are correctly not collapsible at all. Consulted flutter-ux-craftsman again before touching code. It rejected giving `CollapsibleSection` the same full `Card` chrome `CollapsibleCard` already uses (Reminders' rows, Insights' Tools and Bigger Picture bands), reasoning that `CollapsibleCard` boxes a PEER among several competing items in a band, while `CollapsibleSection` is still a page-level heading, just a tappable one; full card chrome on every Menu section would make the whole first screenful look like a stack of empty accordion boxes before any content shows, trading one "not appealing" problem for another, and would blur the real distinction (band-peer vs page-section) rather than the intended one (tappable vs not). Landed on a lighter, token-only fix scoped to `CollapsibleSection`'s header alone: wrapped the `Row` in a `Container` with `Barako.surfaceRaised` fill, a `Barako.border` outline, `Radii.sm` (10) corners, and `Gap.md`/`Gap.xs` padding, plus recoloring the chevron from `Barako.muted` to `Barako.primary` (the accent orange) for a stronger interactive signal at zero asset cost. Every token reused an existing one already read elsewhere in the app (`surfaceRaised` already means "sits a step above the background," used at `Radii.lg` in `menu.dart`'s own Ask Pan banner); nothing new was invented. MONEY, HELPERS-when-open, PRIVACY, and YOUR DATA were deliberately left as bare `Kicker`s, unchanged: touching them would have been scope creep on a defect that lived in exactly one widget, and the whole point of the fix is a LANGUAGE a user learns once ("a tinted bordered chip with an orange chevron means tap me, a bare label means it is just a heading") and then reads instantly everywhere it recurs, which only works if the two states stay visually distinct. VERIFICATION: `dart format` clean, `flutter analyze` 0 issues, full `flutter test` suite green at 2103 tests (unchanged count, a pure styling change, no new logic). `test/palette_contrast_test.dart` passed, the relevant automated proof that a new bordered surface does not introduce a contrast regression across any of the app's sixteen palettes, not just the one rendered and looked at. `test/a11y_test.dart` also passed, confirming the added `Container` padding did not shrink the tap target back under Android's 48dp minimum (the exact bug f3.40 itself introduced and fixed). Also individually reran `test/screen_readability_test.dart`, `test/menu_grid_test.dart`, `test/name_ask_test.dart`, `test/currency_test.dart`, `test/mood_theme_test.dart`, and `test/widget_privacy_test.dart`, all of which touch Menu's collapsible rows in some way, all green with no changes needed. Learning directly from f3.40's own CI-only failure (a golden-render file with no `_test.dart` suffix that never runs as part of the default `flutter test` sweep), `test/screens_shot.dart --update-goldens` was run and checked SEPARATELY this time, deliberately, before treating the change as verified: 60 of 60 passed. Rendered and looked at directly, dark and light, scrolled to the collapsed REMINDERS and SECURITY rows: both now read as a clearly distinct, tinted, bordered, rounded chip with an orange chevron, immediately different from the flat PRIVACY label sitting right beneath them, which is exactly the comparison the founder's own screenshot was making. Two scratch-only renders (written, viewed, then deleted, never committed). NOT DONE: no qa-tester agent pass (a pure visual/styling change, no money math, storage, or security surface touched); no new automated test added, since this is styling on an already-tested interactive widget and the existing a11y, contrast, and readability sweeps already cover the properties that could regress. |
| f3.42 | flutter-ui-polish skill loaded for this pass; implementation and verification done directly, no qa-tester pass, a screen-architecture change to Menu | 0 new defects; the founder's own repeated dissatisfaction across f3.40 and f3.41 was the actual finding, not something an agent or test caught first | THE STEP: f3.40 gave Menu whole-section in-place collapse (CollapsibleSection); f3.41 restyled the collapsed header into a tinted, bordered chip after the founder said it still read as flat. The founder then attached a screenshot of the RN app's own More tab (mobile/app/(tabs)/more.js) and said, plainly, that they were still not satisfied, and to adapt or improve on THAT design instead. Read the RN source directly rather than guessing: More tab's SETTINGS card is four short `Pressable` rows (Theme and colors, Notifications and security, Preferences, Backup and data), each showing a one-line current-value blurb and a chevron, each pushing its OWN destination screen (`/appearance`, `/notifications`, `/preferences`, `/data`) rather than expanding anything in place. `mobile/app/notifications.js`'s own header comment says the quiet part out loud: these settings "used to sit inline... and made it a very long scroll," and moving them to a real screen, not collapsing them, was RN's actual fix for the same problem Flutter had been trying to solve with an accordion for two stamps running. ARCHITECTURE CHANGE: Menu's PERSONALIZE, YOUR NAME, REMINDERS, and SECURITY Kicker sections are gone. In their place, one bare `Kicker('SETTINGS')` plus four `_navRow` entries (Appearance, Currency, Your name, Notifications and security), the exact same boxed-card-plus-chevron widget already used for Privacy receipt, Diagnostics, and New phone day, so these four rows are now visually and structurally IDENTICAL to destinations the founder had never flagged as confusing. This eliminates the whole "does this look tappable" question `CollapsibleSection` kept failing, by removing the need for a bespoke interactive language altogether. `CollapsibleSection` itself is deleted from `widgets/section.dart` as now-dead code (`CollapsibleCard`, Insights' own unrelated widget, is untouched). A NEW SCREEN, `lib/screens/notifications_security.dart`, holds the Reminders card (all nine rows, unchanged, still using the existing per-row `_ReminderRow` collapse internally, since that part of f3.39 was never the complaint) plus App lock and the widget-privacy switch, reached by tapping the "Notifications and security" row; `_remindersCard`, `_appLockCard`, `_widgetPrivacyCard`, and the `_ReminderRow` class all moved out of `menu.dart` into this file, unmodified in substance. YOUR NAME interaction changed shape too: the old inline card's separate Remove/Set/Change text buttons collapsed into one `_nameRow` (title, current name or "Not set" as the blurb, tap opens the edit dialog directly); Remove moved INTO that dialog's own actions, shown only when a name currently exists, so one row now carries all three actions that used to compete for space on the card. TESTS REWORKED (six files, all reverting or replacing the collapse-era interaction with the new push-navigation one): `name_ask_test.dart` (tap "Your name" opens the dialog directly, Remove found inside it), `mood_theme_test.dart` and `currency_test.dart` (no more PERSONALIZE-expand step, Appearance and Currency are directly visible again), `widget_privacy_test.dart` (navigates into the new Notifications and security screen first), `screen_readability_test.dart` (the new screen added to its sweep map, its file-list assertion, and its swept-files set, since that file's own guard fails loudly on any screen file that is neither swept nor exempted with a reason, and caught this immediately), `screens_shot.dart` (the Reminders shot now navigates into the new screen first; the Your name shot repurposed to capture the edit dialog itself, since that is now the state carrying the most to get wrong, three actions instead of the row's old two). VERIFICATION: `dart format` clean, `flutter analyze` 0 issues, full `flutter test` suite green at 2105 tests (up 2 from 2103, the new screen file's own coverage entries). `screen_readability_test.dart`'s "Notifications and security" entry passed at both 1.0x and 1.5x system font, confirming the new screen holds up under dynamic type. `screens_shot.dart --update-goldens` checked SEPARATELY from the default sweep, deliberately, learning from an earlier PR's own CI-only failure this session: 60 of 60 passed. Rendered and looked at directly, dark and light: the new SETTINGS card (four rows, indistinguishable in style from Privacy receipt below them), the new Notifications and security screen (Reminders content unchanged, reachable and scrollable), and the Your name edit dialog with a name set (Remove, Cancel, Save fit on one line with no overflow). Four scratch-only renders (written, viewed, then deleted, never committed). NOT DONE: no qa-tester agent pass (a screen-architecture change, not money math, storage, or a new security surface, since App lock and the widget-privacy switch kept their exact existing logic, only their location moved); no new automated test for the SETTINGS card's row structure itself, since `_navRow` is already the most exercised, already-tested widget in this file. |
| f3.43 | one flutter-ux-craftsman consultation (read-only, no code access; implementation and verification done directly, no qa-tester pass, a presentation-only change to already-computed numbers) plus a ruled-out hypothesis confirmed by direct rendering | 0 new defects; 1 real chrome bug fixed as a side effect (a double-boxed card), the founder's own feedback was the actual finding | THE STEP: founder attached a screenshot of the Insights tab with all nine cards (TOOLS' four, THE BIGGER PICTURE's five) collapsed to identical `CollapsibleCard` chevron rows, no numbers, no chart, nothing to glance at, and said plainly: "I think it should be expanded... I will not make the user overwhelmed." FIRST RULED OUT A DIFFERENT HYPOTHESIS: before touching content, checked whether the Log floating action button was permanently covering the last visible row (a real defect class, distinct from the content question). Rendered the exact same screen scrolled to its true resting bottom at the founder's own device resolution (1440x2783, a shorter and more square ratio than the 1170x2532 canvas every other render in this repo uses) and at three system font scales (1.0x, 1.15x, 1.3x); never reproduced a persistent overlap at rest, only what is clearly a normal mid-scroll frame where a FAB briefly sits over whatever content is currently underneath it, which is expected Material behavior. Concluded the complaint was about the CONTENT, not a layout defect, and said so before continuing. CONSULTED flutter-ux-craftsman with that conclusion and the founder's own preference already in hand, asking for a concrete design, not a menu. It read what each of the nine cards actually computes when expanded and recommended splitting the two bands rather than treating them the same: TOOLS stays collapsed, unchanged, since the code's own existing comment already justifies it correctly (opt-in input tools, "used to render fully open, two permanent screenfuls of input fields"); THE BIGGER PICTURE should stop being collapsible at all, since every one of its five cards already computes a real hero the moment it builds, a percent and progress bar (`_spokenForCard`), an "XX of 100" score with four sub-bars (`_healthCard`), a six month income-versus-spending chart (`_trendCard`), a spend line and category bars (`_categoriesCard`), a runway sentence (`_runwayCard`), and collapsing that band hid the entire reason the tab exists. IMPLEMENTED exactly that in `flutter/lib/screens/insights.dart`: removed the five `CollapsibleCard` wrappers around `_spokenForCard`/`_healthCard`/`_trendCard`/`_categoriesCard`/`_runwayCard` (the call site at the "THE BIGGER PICTURE" band, roughly lines 406-436), calling each function directly instead so they always render open. A `Kicker(title)` plus its matching spacing was added back as the first child inside each of those five functions, restoring the exact original ALL-CAPS titles ('SPOKEN FOR EACH MONTH', 'MONEY HEALTH', 'LAST 6 MONTHS', 'WHERE YOUR MONEY WENT THIS MONTH', 'EMERGENCY RUNWAY') that used to live in the `CollapsibleCard` header and would otherwise have been silently lost. THE SIDE-EFFECT BUG FIXED: `CollapsibleCard`'s own header renders as a separate boxed `Card` stacked above the child's own `Card` whenever expanded (`widgets/section.dart`), so the OLD default-collapsed Bigger Picture, once a card was opened, actually showed two visually separate boxed cards with a gap between them for what reads as one logical card; removing the wrapper collapses that back into a single, correctly-boxed card, a real visual defect nobody had reported yet because almost nobody had tapped one open to see it. TOOLS is untouched: still four `CollapsibleCard` rows, still collapsed by default, still correct for opt-in utilities the code's own comment already explains. TESTS REWORKED: `test/insights_screen_test.dart`, three separate spots that used to scroll to a `CollapsibleCard` header string like "Your money health score" or "How much of your salary is spoken for" and tap it open before checking the number or chart labels inside; all three now scroll to the restored ALL-CAPS Kicker text directly with no tap, since the content is simply already there. VERIFICATION: `dart format` clean, `flutter analyze` 0 issues, full `flutter test` suite green at 2105 tests (unchanged count, a presentation-only change to already-computed and already-tested numbers). `screens_shot.dart --update-goldens` checked separately from the default sweep, now standing practice this session after an earlier PR's own CI-only failure: 60 of 60 passed. `test/palette_contrast_test.dart` and `test/a11y_test.dart` both green, confirming the newly-always-visible cards introduce no contrast or tap-target regression. Rendered and looked at directly, dark and light: the Bigger Picture band now shows real figures immediately on scroll, no taps needed, ₱15,649 spoken for with its own progress bar, an 84 of 100 health score broken into four labeled sub-bars, a six-month income-versus-spending line chart with a legend, each inside one single, correctly-boxed card. NOT DONE: no qa-tester agent pass (a presentation-only change, no money math, storage, or security surface touched, every figure shown was already computed and already tested by the golden analytics engines); no new automated test for the always-open rendering itself, since the existing content and readability sweeps already cover what could regress. |
| f3.44 | legal-compliance-counsel (task-scoped: one read-only regulatory reviewer against exactly the four official sources named in the task, no qa-tester or additional agent, per this phase's own explicit agent budget), plus thorough self-review and self-test against every requirement in the task | 1 medium finding (a missing incontestability-clause line in the read-the-policy checklist) fixed and re-checked; 1 low, deferred verification item (VUL charge-category wording against CL 2017-34's own text, blocked by a 403 on every direct fetch of insurance.gov.ph in this environment); the deliberate omission of a specific cooling-off day count was confirmed correct, not a gap | THE STEP: Money Courses Phase 9, "Protect Your Future"'s first course, "Insurance Decoded" (`lib/content/lessons_insurance.dart`), a new second learning path alongside Grow Your Money. Six lessons (What Insurance Is For, Start With the Protection Need, Term and Whole-Life Insurance, VUL Without the Sales Pitch, Read the Policy Before Signing, Verify Compare and Decide), registered in `lib/content/learning_paths.dart` as `protect_your_future` with one group, `insurance_decoded`; `lessonsForPath` and `expansionLessonById` each got the matching branch, the same closed-switch pattern the Grow Your Money courses already use. Two new Salapify-action routes, `recurring` and `notifications`, added to `widgets/expansion_lesson_reader.dart`'s existing closed route switch for this course's "add a recurring premium" and "open reminders" actions, exactly the way Phase 7A added `mindset` and `accounts`; every prior route resolves unchanged. Built entirely on the already-shipped Phase 2-5 architecture (governance metadata, official-source/risk-warning/educational-boundary blocks, interaction blocks); no new interaction block type. Lessons 1-4 classified `ContentVolatility.annual`; lessons 5 and 6 (cooling-off rights, agent licensing) classified `ContentVolatility.high` with a shorter review cycle, per the task's own instruction to treat regulatory, licensed-agent, consumer-rights, and cooling-off content as time-sensitive. AGENT REVIEW: legal-compliance-counsel read all six lessons against the four sources the task named (RA 10607, CL 2017-34, the RA 11765 IRR, and insurance.gov.ph); every direct `WebFetch` of those four URLs returned 403 in this environment, so findings are grounded in `WebSearch`-indexed secondary legal commentary, disclosed explicitly rather than presented as a first-hand primary read. Confirmed clean: no real insurer, agent, or product named anywhere; the "license does not guarantee suitability" and "term versus whole-life has no universal winner" framings hold exactly as required; the Lesson 6 verification guidance matches how the Commission's own site actually operates. FINDING (medium, fixed): RA 10607 Section 227's incontestability clause, a mandatory provision in every individual life policy, was absent from Lesson 5's "where to look in a policy" walkthrough and questions checklist. | f3.49 | legal-compliance-counsel (task-scoped: one read-only regulatory reviewer specifically asked to scrutinize this course's own deliberate exception to the usual no-hardcoded-figure rule, rigorously, points A through E named explicitly in the task) plus my own independent WebSearch verification of the five official sources and the RA 11976 fee-abolition facts before trusting the claim | 1 MUST FIX (a wrong year, 2026 instead of 2024, in the sentence introducing this course's flagship figure) fixed and re-verified before merge; 2 low SHOULD FIX polish notes (a review-cycle observation, a wording-consistency fix) both applied; 0 other findings | THE STEP: Money Courses Phase 14, "Build Your Business"'s second course, "BIR Registration and Local Permits" (`lib/content/lessons_bir_local_permits.dart`, course id `bir_registration_and_local_permits`), a NEW course authored this session (not pre-authored by an earlier commit), in direct response to the founder's own request for an ordered guide to Philippine business compliance (DTI, then BIR, then local permits, "so users would know where to start"). Six lessons picking up exactly where Phase 13's "Start Your Business Legally" left off: the general order of remaining steps, BIR registration (TIN via Form 1901/1902/1903, Certificate of Registration Form 2303), books of accounts and invoices, barangay clearance and the city or municipal Business Permit, taxpayer size classification, and a closing compliance calendar. Registered as this path's second `LearningPathGroup`, so `build_your_business` now holds 12 lessons across two courses. THE DELIBERATE EXCEPTION, founder-approved before writing a single lesson (see the AskUserQuestion exchange this same turn): unlike every earlier expansion course's strict "never hardcode a volatile figure" rule, this course states four real national figures (the ₱500 Annual Registration Fee's abolition under RA 11976/EOPT effective January 22 2024, a ~₱30 Documentary Stamp Tax that still applies once, Authority to Print now free, and the invoice-issuance threshold rising from ₱100 to ₱500), each classified `ContentVolatility.high` with a review due in roughly 6 months rather than the usual year, each sitting beside a `RiskWarningBlock` naming that the rule can change. It deliberately NEVER states a figure for barangay or city/municipal Business Permit fees, since those are not merely time-volatile but structurally set independently by more than 1,600 separate local governments, a fact taught directly via a dedicated MythOrFactBlock ("the Business Permit fee is the same everywhere" = MYTH). AGENT REVIEW AND WHAT IT CAUGHT: legal-compliance-counsel was asked to be unusually rigorous given this exception, specifically to verify each of the four stated figures independently, confirm the local-fee omission was reasoning rather than a gap, and judge whether the hedging (6-month review, RiskWarningBlock, "confirm before paying" language) was sufficiently protective. It found and fixed ONE MUST FIX: a sentence introducing the annual-fee-abolition fact read "As of January 22, 2026," a wrong year (the law took effect January 22, 2024; every other place in the same lesson, the myth block, the knowledge check, all correctly said 2024). This is exactly the class of error the review step exists to catch in a course whose entire safety case rests on stated figures being accurate. Fixed to remove the wrong date and state the correct one, re-verified with a fresh test run. Two low SHOULD FIX notes, both applied: matched Lesson 3's hedge wording ("before setting anything up") to Lesson 2's stronger "before paying anything" phrasing for consistency; and a review-cycle observation (BIR issued several implementing rules within months of RA 11976, so a quarterly review would be more conservative than 6 months) noted for the next cycle, not changed now since the current window plus explicit hedge language was judged adequately protective. CONFIRMED CORRECT via independent WebSearch, both by the reviewer and by me separately: all four stated figures are accurate (₱500 fee abolition, ~₱30 DST, free ATP, ₱100 to ₱500 threshold); the local-fee omission is correctly reasoned, Philippine LGU permit fees really do vary independently by ordinance with no fixed national floor (rooted in the Local Government Code of 1991); taxpayer-classification gross-sales thresholds exist and are currently stable under RR 8-2024 but the course's own reason for omitting them (a reader's own classification depends on numbers the course can't know) holds independent of that stability, so no change needed; all five official sources (BIR's EOPT page, BIR's forms directory, ORUS, DTI's registration-and-permits page, and the reused Philippine Business Hub page) are real, on the correct domain, and cover the topic cited. NOT ONE WORD of lesson content was edited and no stored data shape changed. (1) THE CITATION STACK (audit H1): each long lesson ended its teaching with three to five grey legal cards, 63 official-source cards and 29 identical boundary paragraphs across one path, landing exactly where a reader decides whether to continue. They now collapse into one line at the end that opens on tap. The rule drawn, once, so both readers agree: a warning TEACHES and stays inline, a citation PROVES and moves. Nothing that matters is hidden, the boundary SENTENCE and every cited agency stay visible while collapsed, and expanding renders the original cards untouched with every URL, issuance number and verification date. (2) HONEST MINUTES: the reader now shows max(authored, computed) so it can never round a lesson DOWN. The four tests that blocked this in Batch 1 were REWRITTEN rather than deleted: they asserted a number on screen as a proxy for the real invariant, and now assert that invariant directly against the store (settings.lessonProgress stays empty and every core track's done-count stays zero after an entire expansion path is completed), which is strictly stronger since a screen can render the right number for the wrong reason. (5) COURSE FUNNEL on the diagnostics screen, the founder's answer to "how do we find out if any of this worked": started, done, used-in-app and never-finished per track and per path, counts only, no lesson named and no date recorded, so the privacy test passes unchanged. Deliberately in diagnostics and not anywhere a learner meets it, because a screen telling someone they abandoned four courses is the app being unkind about the thing they already feel bad about. THREE DEFECTS THE TESTS CAUGHT, worth recording as beliefs rather than typos: (a) the first reading-time model used a flat per-interaction allowance, so exercise text was invisible to the counter and the very lesson the fix exists for still estimated at its authored 6 minutes; interaction text is now counted through an exhaustive switch, so a new block type is a compile error rather than a silent zero. (b) The assertion that the core 22 would be unchanged was my assumption, not a requirement: the audit's "194 words" is an AVERAGE, `freelancer-setaside` is 453 words because it carries the dense 8 percent tax explanation, and past two minutes is simply true, so the guard now asserts no core lesson drifts more than a minute plus a NAMED list of the one that moves. (c) The Home card was first placed only in the has-started branch, so a brand new user, exactly the person a two-minute lesson helps most, would never have seen it; the widget test caught it before commit. ALSO CAUGHT AND UNDONE: an earlier draft of the first commit swept 143 unrelated files through the current `dart format`, because `dart format lib test` reformats everything a newer SDK styles differently; neither workflow gates on formatting and that sweep would have collided with the parallel branch for no benefit, so it was dropped and only the eight touched files shipped. The measurement was wrong, not the content. Fixed to split on any real terminator, at which point all three were comfortably under the cap. A checker that reports a fault which is not there wastes exactly as much time as one that misses a real fault, and had I trusted it I would have rewritten three perfectly good paragraphs to satisfy a bug. Three breaks: (1) the prose chunk end index off by one, which reddened the paragraph-preservation guard with `Expected: ['p0'..'p6'] / Actual: ['p0','p2','p4','p6']`, a silent data-loss bug that pagination could easily have shipped; (2) the reference-block `continue` removed, so citations claimed their own screens mid lesson (`Expected: <1> / Actual: <3>`); (3) `stepGatesProgress` forced to false, which reddened BOTH the unit guard (`Expected: true / Actual: <false>`) and the widget guard (`Found 0 widgets with text "Finish this activity to continue."`), proving the gate is enforced in the reader and not only in the model | 0 must-fix; 2 real test failures caught by the full suite and fixed before merge, both the correct kind: two deep-link tests still asserted the SCROLLING reader after LearnScreen started opening the paged one, which is the tests noticing a genuine behaviour change rather than a defect | THE STEP: Phase 3, the structural half of the founder's original complaint. An expansion lesson was one long scroll; it is now a short sequence of screens with one Continue button. `lib/money/lesson_steps.dart` (pure, 13 tests) decides where the breaks fall: prose chunked at two paragraphs with the heading riding only the first, each exercise alone on its own screen so it cannot be scrolled past, the quiz its own screen, and citations riding the finish screen rather than ever taking one of their own. `lib/widgets/paged_lesson_reader.dart` walks that list and reuses every existing block view unchanged, so NO lesson content moved and no block was restyled. A step progress bar sits under the app bar, announced to a screen reader as "Step 3 of 9" rather than as a bare percentage. WHAT DELIBERATELY DID NOT CHANGE, because these are the promises earlier batches made: opening never finishes a lesson; a finished lesson opens finished (audit C6); only a REQUIRED interaction gates, and it now gates the screen it lives on rather than a button far below it; the quiz never gates; confetti fires for a finished course or path and never a lesson. Free swiping is disabled on purpose, since a swipe would slide a reader straight past the one exercise the gate exists to stop them skipping. ALSO: `LessonFinishCard` extracted so the finish moment exists once rather than three times, and the expansion action-route resolver promoted from private so both readers resolve the identical closed set, since two readers with two route tables is how one of them quietly grows a dead button. The scrolling `ExpansionLessonReader` is RETAINED and still fully tested; `LearnScreen` opens the paged one, so there is a working fallback one line away until the founder has confirmed this on a real phone. It now has five cases covering question marks, exclamation marks, full stops, single sentences, and one PINNED known limitation (an abbreviation splits early), so a future opening that introduces `e.g.` fails there with an obvious cause instead of quietly miscounting. No information was lost, and the retro separately verified byte-identical dumps of all 29 lessons outside the opening plus every figure and proper noun surviving, but "kept every item" overstated what a reader counting list items would see. NOT DONE, deliberately: exercises still arrive after the reading rather than interleaved among it, because nothing in the content declares WHICH idea a given exercise tests and any automatic interleaving would be a guess dressed as pedagogy; that waits until a lesson can declare it. | f3.58 | qa-tester (self-run, read-only pass over the two new screens, the pure module change, and every call site the removed accordion touched); plus a deliberate double break-then-prove on the two screen guards | 2 defects found by the render and fixed before merge, both invisible to the module tests; 1 test-accounting invariant correctly reddened by the new file and repaired rather than loosened | THE STEP: Money Courses Phase 4, catalog navigation. Tapping "All courses" on an expansion path (Grow Your Money, Protect Your Future, Build Your Business) used to inject about thirty two-line lesson rows into the middle of the Learn hub scroll: the collapse control scrolled away under the reader, the position jumped when it closed, and there was no way to reach ONE course at all. A learner could not open "Crypto Without the Hype"; they could only open the whole path blob and hunt for it. The experience audit filed this as H3. The catalog now nests the way a learner already thinks about it, path then course then lesson: `lib/screens/path_screen.dart` holds `PathScreen` (one path, its courses as cards, each with its own progress bar, its own count, and a button saying Start, Continue or Read again) and `CourseScreen` (one course, its lessons, with a progress line, per-lesson state, position and honest minutes). The hub keeps its inline expansion for the CORE four tracks, deliberately: six rows in a card is exactly the size that pattern was right for, and pushing a screen for six rows would be ceremony. New pure API in `money/lesson_flow.dart`: `courseProgress()` derives per-course counts from the same ordered sequence both readers already pass, and `focusCourseId()` decides which single course gets the loud button. WHAT THE RENDER CAUGHT, and the module tests could not: the first draft gave EVERY course card a filled accent button, which rendered as five identical orange slabs down the page with nothing for the eye to follow, the same "everything is emphasised so nothing is" note the audit made about the hub; and `CourseScreen` was the only screen in the path-course-lesson chain with no progress line at all, so walking one level in lost the figure entirely. Both were obvious at a glance in the PNG and invisible to a green suite, which is the whole reason the look-at-the-screen rule exists. `focusCourseId` prefers a STARTED-but-unfinished course over an untouched earlier one, because resuming something half-read is a smaller ask than starting a new subject; that preference is the only behaviour in the function a plain "first unfinished" rule would get wrong, so it has the test that can tell the two rules apart. BREAK-THEN-PROVE: the resume preference was deleted and `focusCourseId a half-read course wins over an untouched earlier one` reported `Expected: 'b' Actual: 'a'`; then, on the screen side, `isFocus` was pinned to `true` and `exactly one course carries the filled button` reported `Found 5 widgets with type "FilledButton" / Which: is too many`, while the course progress line was pinned to zero and `the course screen counts a finished lesson` reported `Found 0 widgets with text "1 of 5 lessons done"`. Each break was restored only after the run had reported. THE ACCOUNTING INVARIANT: `screen_readability_test.dart` asserts one swept entry per file in `lib/screens`, and `path_screen.dart` holds two real destinations, so the new file reddened it at 37 pumped vs 36 claimed. Repaired by DECLARING the extra face in a named map with a reason, not by loosening the comparison to an inequality: "at least as many pumped as claimed" would also pass a file that quietly stopped being pumped while another gained a second face. NOT DONE, deliberately: the core four tracks keep the accordion (correct at six rows); Protect Your Future and Build Your Business lesson openings still unrewritten; exercise interleaving still waits on content that can declare which idea an exercise tests. |
| f3.59 | lunch-and-learn agent (the f3.57 retrospective, which found this defect on SHIPPED code and reproduced it twice); plus self-verification of the mechanism before acting on the report, and a double break-then-prove on the two new guards | 1 real defect on shipped code, fixed structurally; 1 second instance of the same class, guarded by test because it cannot be fixed structurally | THE DEFECT, found by a retrospective rather than by a test: Phase 3 (f3.57) moved two decisions from INSIDE the expansion lesson reader out to its call site in `learn.dart`, and neither absence is loud. `interaction_block_views.dart` falls back to `resolveRoute: resolveSalapifyRoute ?? (_) => null`, and `lesson_finish_card.dart` guards with `if (next != null && onOpenLesson != null)`. So deleting the wiring in `learn.dart` removes a working button from the phone and leaves the entire suite green. The retrospective ran both deletions on shipped code: the route one passed 2,623 tests, the next-lesson one passed all nine reader and Learn test files, and `git grep resolveSalapifyRoute -- flutter/test` returned nothing at all. I verified the mechanism myself before acting rather than taking the report at face value: `ExpansionLessonReader` resolves routes inline at line 262 (default-on, free to every widget test), `PagedLessonReader` took it as an optional constructor parameter supplied only by `learn.dart` (default-off, invisible). ROOT CAUSE, structural not attentional: a dependency moved from default-on to default-off whose absent value is a DEGRADED APP rather than an error is invisible by construction, and the f3.57 commit message shows the author reasoning about exactly this risk while solving only the duplication half of it. THE FIX, in two different shapes because the two halves are not the same problem: routes are now unrepresentably-missing, since `PagedLessonReader` resolves them itself through the same `resolveExpansionActionRoute` closed table the scrolling reader has always used inline, so the two readers can never drift into different sets of live buttons and a caller that forgets loses nothing; `onOpenLesson` CANNOT be defaulted internally, because it has to push a screen and the reader must not import screens, so it is guarded by a test at the app own call site instead. TESTS, and an honest note about what the second one does not do: `lesson_reader_wiring_test.dart` builds a reader with NO resolver at all, on a deliberately synthetic two-block lesson, and drives it to the actions step and through to `GoalsScreen`. Synthetic on purpose: every real expansion lesson gates its actions step behind a required exercise, so a real-content version would spend its length satisfying whichever exercise that lesson happens to author and would break on an edit unrelated to route wiring. The second test opens a lesson through the real hub (All courses, course card, lesson row) and asserts the reader was handed a non-null `onOpenLesson`. That is a WIRING check, not a click-through, and the file says so in its own header rather than implying more: it cannot tell you the Next button looks right, only that it exists at all, which is exactly what was silently deleted. BREAK THEN PROVE, both halves, restored only after the run reported and `git diff` confirmed `learn.dart` came back byte-identical: removing the internal default reported `Found 0 widgets with type "OutlinedButton"` under the reason string `the action rendered as text with no button, which is exactly what a null route resolver produces`; nulling the hub wiring reported `Expected: not null  Actual: <null>` under `the hub opened a lesson without giving the reader any way to open the next one`. ALSO FIXED while writing the tests, and worth recording because it is a trap: `scrollUntilVisible` stops once a widget is BUILT, which on a lazy list is not the same as being inside the viewport. It reported success at y=923 on an 844 tall frame and the tap then missed the render tree entirely; `ensureVisible` is what actually brings it on screen. NOT DONE, deliberately: the retrospective also noted that the old scrolling reader carried 1.5x overflow and semantics tests the paged reader never inherited, and that a paged version must tap through EVERY step since a PageView builds one page at a time. Real, and a bigger piece of work than this fix; recorded here and not silently dropped. Session 34 qa-log staleness check still unbuilt, now carried a fourth session. |

| f3.61 | lunch-and-learn agent (session 37, which found the stale shot map); plus independent verification of its central claim before acting (17 vs 1, and nothing in lib constructing the scrolling reader), and break-then-prove on all three new guards including one break that DID NOT FIRE and had to be redone | 1 stale-harness defect fixed at its root, 2 missing accessibility checks built, 1 guard-design limit found by a failed break and documented rather than hidden | THE DEFECT, and it is about the tool rather than the app: the render harness built `ExpansionLessonReader` in 17 lesson shots and `PagedLessonReader` in one, while `lib` stopped constructing the scrolling reader entirely at f3.57. Seventeen careful pictures pointed at a widget a learner could no longer open, and were reviewed by the founder as evidence about shipped screens. Worse, the ONLY 1.5x overflow test and the ONLY semantics test on the whole lesson surface pumped that same unreachable widget, so the reader people actually use had neither check for three stamps. The trigger for going back was written down in learn.dart ("stays until this has been confirmed on a real phone") and the confirmation happened three times; a condition nobody is scheduled to re-read is not a plan. I verified the counts myself before acting rather than taking the retrospective at face value. THE GUARD, built first so it could be watched going red on the real problem: `test/shot_reachability_test.dart` reads the shot map, extracts every widget class it constructs, and fails when one is not constructed anywhere in `lib`. Its first two drafts REPORTED FALSE ALARMS and both were fixed rather than exempted, which is the part worth recording: `Milestone` (a plain data class, not a screen) was excluded by restricting the check to StatelessWidget and StatefulWidget subclasses; `PersonSheet` (declared and shown in the same file, entirely legitimate) and `OnboardingScreen` (built inside a ternary in main.dart) forced the check to tell a constructor DECLARATION from a USE by what precedes it. A guard that names live screens as dead fills its exemption map with excuses and then nobody believes the one entry that is real, so the exemption map ships EMPTY. THE FIX AT THE ROOT, not the exemption: all 17 shots now build the paged reader, via a `lessonShot` helper deliberately taking the same named arguments so the migration was one mechanical rename rather than 17 hand edits that could each have pointed at the wrong lesson. A naive rename would have been worse than nothing: a paged reader opens on prose, so all 17 would have become pictures of first screens, which is exactly the "tidy shot of an empty screen" the render rule warns about. So `PagedLessonReader` gained an `initialStep` (clamped, documented, zero for every real learner) and the harness computes each shot step from the content via `firstExerciseStep`, which THROWS rather than falling back to step zero. Three shots were opened and looked at: the bond timeline, the loss simulator and the readiness card all land on their intended widget. THE MISSING COVERAGE: `test/paged_lesson_reader_a11y_test.dart` sweeps EVERY step of three lessons at 1.5x on a 320dp phone for text running off the side, for RenderFlex overflow, and for a blank page, plus the step announcement on every step and the myth/fact labels. Copying the old tests across would not have closed the gap and that is the whole point: a PageView builds one page at a time, so a copied test measures the opening paragraph and reports nine unseen screens as safe. BREAK THEN PROVE, four breaks, three fired and ONE DID NOT: pinning the step label to "Step 1" reported `Found 0 widgets with a semantics label named "Step 2 of 8"`; the guard reported `ExpansionLessonReader` by name before the migration; a Row-and-fixed-width break inside SortingView reported at `step 5 of 10`, which is itself the proof that the per-step sweep reaches deep steps rather than re-measuring step one. The break that did NOT fire was a sixty character unbreakable word in an exercise kicker, and per the house rule it was not shrugged off: a long word does not widen a Text render box past its constraints, it paints outside it, so neither check can see that shape AT ALL. That blind spot is now written into the test file rather than left implied, because a check whose limit is undocumented gets trusted for things it never did. Every break was restored only after its run reported, and `git diff` confirms `interaction_block_views.dart` came back byte identical. ALSO FOUND, and it is a trap not a defect: pumping the reader in a loop reuses the State, whose `_index` is a `late` field initialised once, so every step after the first rendered step one while the test cheerfully claimed a full sweep. Fixed with a per-step Key and documented. Not an app defect, since a learner gets a fresh route each time. NOT DONE, deliberately: `ExpansionLessonReader` still EXISTS in lib as dead code with three test files pumping it. Deleting it is a real piece of work (roughly 800 lines of behaviour tests would need porting to a paged shape, not renaming) and doing it badly would lose coverage this batch just spent effort creating. The shot map no longer points at it, which is the harm that was actually reaching the founder. Session 34 qa-log staleness check still unbuilt, fifth session. |
| f3.63 | security-privacy-auditor, data-migration-reviewer, and flutter-ux-craftsman agents (parallel, read-only), plus self-review and a look-at-the-screen pass in dark and light | 4 must-fix found, all FIXED and re-checked, 0 remaining. SECURITY (verdict BLOCKED -> resolved): (1) the free-text note fields accepted anything while the screen promised "never the full number", so a person could paste a card number into Notes and it would ride the plaintext backup; now any Luhn-valid 13 to 19 digit run is redacted before storage (money/account_taxonomy.dart `_redactCardNumbers`, tested with the 4111 1111 1111 1111 PAN kept vs a 12-digit reference kept), and the on-screen copy is honest advice rather than a false guarantee. (2) Start fresh and Restore left the QR image files on disk until the next launch's best-effort sweep; both now sweep synchronously against the fresh keep-set (menu.dart, overview.dart). UX/ACCESSIBILITY (2 fix-now): (3) the card network wordmark was Positioned bottom-right and overlapped the credit utilization bar and the "of [limit]" text; moved onto the chip row well clear of the footer. (4) the hero card printed the last four in the clear and read it to a screen reader as a run of bullets; now wrapped in Semantics/ExcludeSemantics that announce "ending 1234" once, and the hero masks the digits until Reveal so the whole screen has one gated place for them. data-migration-reviewer verdict: SAFE TO MERGE, 0 must-fix (every new key conditional and validated-then-dropped, RN backups round-trip byte-identical, the store patch allowlist cannot touch money or identity). ALSO fixed while testing: a real production hang where a QR FutureBuilder created its file-read future inline in build, re-reading forever and never settling (now the future is made once in a StatefulWidget); and a FLAG_SECURE race where a background store notify on an App-Lock-off phone could clear the secure flag mid-reveal (SecureWindow now carries a force-latch refcount that reveal and the QR viewer hold, and the reveal re-hides on background). Break-then-prove on the reveal guard: with the fake authenticator returning false the digits stay hidden and the prompt count is 1, proving the gate fires AND stays silent when refused. | THE STEP: a wallet-style Account Detail screen, reached by tapping any card, plus credit-card network selection, an optional masked last-4 with an authenticated reveal, and a local QR-code vault. New: money/card_products.dart (five networks and per-issuer generic-tier profiles for the confirmed PH credit-card issuers, researched against 2026 sources, unverified issuers carry a generic offering only, plus a QR-receiving hint set), data/qr_vault.dart (a pure, testable vault: images are device-local files referenced by a vault-owned filename only, never a path, orphans swept on startup and on delete/replace/erase), screens/account_detail.dart (the screen: hero BankCard, an Overview panel, a Secure information section with reveal/copy behind device auth, a Receiving QR section, and recent activity), and the tests. Extended: money/account_taxonomy.dart and data/backup.dart add cardNetwork/cardProductId/annualFee (debts only) and accountHolderName/paymentInstructions/branchDetails/qrLabel/qrRef/sensitiveDataProtectionVersion as CONDITIONAL keys, every one validated and dropped when junk so the backup goldens stay green; store.dart gains patchAccountMeta/patchAssetMeta/patchDebtMeta with a tight allowlist that can never reach balance, id, name, kind, remaining or subtype; bank_card.dart draws the network wordmark (text, never a logo); debts.dart's credit-card form gains an institution picker, a network picker adapted to the issuer, a last-4 field and an annual-fee field with the "save only the last four" safety copy; accounts.dart routes a card tap to the detail screen. The ONLY digits stored anywhere are the four of last4; no field accepts a full number, CVV, PIN, OTP or password, and the notes redaction enforces it. QR images are device-local by design and never embedded in a backup export, which the on-screen notice states. TESTS: `flutter analyze` 0 issues; new account_metadata_test.dart (metadata validation, backup round-trip both directions, the store patch guards for accounts and debts, the Luhn note redaction, the card catalogue, and the QR vault lifecycle including path-traversal refusal and orphan cleanup) and account_detail_test.dart (the reveal fires and stays silent on refusal, reveals with no lock, the empty and populated QR states, and archive); the two detail faces joined the readability sweep at 1.0x and 1.5x and the shot harness, rendered and looked at in dark and light and sent to the founder. This is the durable guard from the f3.65 lunch-and-learn (docs/lunch-and-learn.md session 38): the render harness `test/screens_shot.dart` is not collected by bare `flutter test`, so a layout change to a shared screen can pass every local test and still break CI, and a harness tap that misses (a target pushed below the fixed viewport) failed silently and surfaced downstream as an illegible `Found 0 widgets with text ...`. Fix: `WidgetController.hitTestWarningShouldBeFatal = true` at the top of the harness `main()`, so any missed tap throws AT the tap site for all 38 of the file's taps at once; CI runs this file as its own step, so it is enforced there unconditionally. Break-then-prove: removing the `ensureVisible` that fixed the transfer shot turned the run red at the tap with `Finder specifies a widget that would not receive pointer events ... Phase 4 batch 3, Budget answers "am I safe". ONE additive engine function, no existing calculation changed: dailyRoom in money/budget.dart spreads summary remaining over the days left in the month (null when no limit or nothing left), unit-tested on fixed vectors, added so the screen never divides money in a widget; documented as the phase's single engine touch. (1) The hierarchy inverts: REMAINING is the hero (AmountText card face) with "left this month" beside it, and spent-of-limit is the caption. The question between kinsenas and katapusan is how much is still safe, not how much is gone. (2) A third state at 85 percent: "Getting close." arrives in WORDS before the money is gone, with the daily pace beside it and the bar tint underlining rather than carrying the state. (3) Over the limit, the overage is the hero with "over your limit" beside it, and the state line names the biggest category as the next cut ("Rent is your biggest category this month, so the next cut counts most there"), replacing "No shame, just ease the biggest category below", which named the shame it disclaimed and gestured instead of instructing. (4) Quick-add receipts state the consequence: the new remaining (engine value recomputed after the write) or the new overage, so every chip tap is a micro budget check. (5) The no-limit copy stops implying dishonesty ("keep you honest" became "show what is left"). (6) TODAY rows adopt the ledger sign grammar through AmountText. (7) The quick-add chips carry a screen-reader hint ("Double tap to log this expense") because activating one writes real money immediately. BREAK-THEN-PROVE, one run, restored after it reported: raising the near threshold to 185 lost "Getting close." from the 88-percent fixture, showing remaining instead of the overage lost the ₱850 hero, and returning undivided remaining from dailyRoom failed the vector (Actual 3100.0 where about 129.17 demanded).
